\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,\left\{2-2\left(1-\frac{H^{2}\left(P_{\varphi(t+h)},P_{\varphi(t)}\right)}{2}\right)^{n}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,\left\{2-2\left(1-\frac{c^{2}h^{2}\int\left\{\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(t+\lambda h)\right\}^{2}\,g_{cn^{-1/2}\varphi_{0}(t)}^{2}\,dP_{cn^{-1/2}\varphi_{0}(t)}+o(c^{2}n^{-1}h^{2})}{8n}\right)^{n}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=2-2\exp\left(-\frac{c^{2}h^{2}\int\left\{\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(t+\lambda h)\right\}^{2}\,g^{2}\,dP_{0}}{8}\right).

Thus we obtain

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,\inf_{T}\,\sup_{|\theta|<cn^{-1/2}}\,n\mathbb{E}_{P_{\theta}^{n}}|T(X)-\psi(\theta)|^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\geq\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,\frac{n|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\psi(P_{\varphi(t)})-\psi(P_{\varphi(t-h)})\right)\,dQ(t)|^{2}}{4H^{2}\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{0},\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h}\right)}-O(c^{2}h^{2})\hskip 9.24994pt\mbox{for all }h\in\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\frac{c^{2}h^{2}\left(\int\dot{\psi}_{0}\,g\,dP_{0}\right)^{2}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(t-\lambda h)\,dQ(t)\right|^{2}+o(c^{2}h^{2})}{4\left(H^{2}(Q_{h},Q_{0})+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left\{2-2\exp\left(-\frac{c^{2}h^{2}\int\left\{\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(t+\lambda h)\right\}^{2}\,g^{2}\,dP_{0}}{8}\right)\right\}\,dQ_{h}^{1/2}\,dQ^{1/2}\right)}-O(c^{2}h^{2})

for all
h\in\mathbb{R}
. Since the above inequality holds for any
h\in\mathbb{R}
, we take
h\longrightarrow 0
to obtain

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,\inf_{T}\,\sup_{|\theta|<cn^{-1/2}}\,n\mathbb{E}_{P_{\theta}^{n}}|T(X)-\psi(\theta)|^{2}~\geq~\frac{c^{2}\left(\int\dot{\psi}_{0}\,g\,dP_{0}\right)^{2}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(t)\,dQ\right|^{2}}{\mathcal{I}(Q)+c^{2}\left(\int g^{2}\,dP_{0}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\{\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(t)\}^{2}\,dQ}.

This follows since

\displaystyle\lim_{h\longrightarrow 0}\,H^{2}(Q_{h},Q_{0})+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left\{2-2\exp\left(-\frac{c^{2}h^{2}\int\left\{\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(t+\lambda h)\right\}^{2}\,g^{2}\,dP_{0}}{8}\right)\right\}\,dQ_{h}^{1/2}\,dQ^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\frac{1}{4}h^{2}\mathcal{I}(Q)+\frac{1}{4}c^{2}h^{2}\left(\int g^{2}\,dP_{0}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left\{\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(t)\right\}^{2}\,dQ

by Taylor expansion. The rest of the proof is identical to the statement (ii) of Proposition
1
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
1
(iv).

Similar to the proof of the statement (iv) of Proposition
3
, we apply Lemma
8
to the QMD parametric paths. Without loss of generality, we assume that
\theta_{0}=0
. Then for fixed
g\in\mathcal{T}_{P_{0}}
, we invoke Lemma
8
as follows:

\displaystyle\sup_{|\theta|<cn^{-1/2}}\,n\mathbb{E}_{P^{n}_{\theta,g}}\big|T(X)-\psi(P_{\theta,g})\big|^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\geq n\sup_{|h|<c}\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathbb{E}_{0}\big|T(X)-\psi(P_{0})\big|^{2}+\mathbb{E}_{hn^{-1/2}}\big|T(X)-\psi(P_{hn^{-1/2}})\big|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\geq n\left[\frac{1-H^{2}(P^{n}_{hn^{-1/2}},P^{n}_{0})}{4}\right]_{+}|\psi(P_{hn^{-1/2}})-\psi(P_{0})|^{2}\hskip 9.24994pt\mbox{for all}\hskip 9.24994pt|h|<c.

Since
\psi
is pathwise differentiable, it follows that

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,n\left|\psi(P_{0})-\psi(P_{hn^{-1/2}})\right|=h^{2}\left(\int\dot{\psi}_{0}\,g\,dP_{0}\right)^{2}.

Also by the QMD assumption of the parametric path, we have

\displaystyle H^{2}(P_{hn^{-1/2}},P_{0})

\displaystyle=\int\left(dP_{hn^{-1/2}}^{1/2}-dP_{0}^{1/2}\right)^{2}=\frac{1}{4}h^{2}n^{-1}\int g^{2}\,dP_{0}+o(h^{2}n^{-1})

followed by the tensorization property of the Hellinger distance,

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}H^{2}(P^{n}_{hn^{-1/2}},P^{n}_{0})

\displaystyle=\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,\left\{2-2\left(1-\frac{H^{2}\left(P_{hn^{-1/2}},P_{0}\right)}{2}\right)^{n}\right\}

\displaystyle=\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,\left\{2-2\left(1-\frac{h^{2}\int g^{2}\,dP_{0}+o(h^{2}n^{-1})}{8n}\right)^{n}\right\}

\displaystyle=2-2\exp\left(-\frac{h^{2}\int g^{2}\,dP_{0}}{8}\right).

Putting them together, we obtain

\displaystyle\liminf_{c\longrightarrow\infty}\,\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,\sup_{|\theta|<cn^{-1/2}}\,n\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\big|T(X)-\psi(P_{\theta})\big|^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\geq\sup_{0\leq h<\infty}\left[-\frac{1}{4}-\frac{1}{2}\exp\left(-\frac{h^{2}\int g^{2}\,dP_{0}}{8}\right)\right]_{+}h^{2}\left(\int\dot{\psi}_{0}\,g\,dP_{0}\right)^{2}.

Similar to the proof of Proposition
3
(iv), we let
\widetilde{h}=h\left(\int g^{2}\,dP_{0}\right)^{1/2}
and optimize over
\widetilde{h}
instead. This yields that

\displaystyle\liminf_{c\longrightarrow\infty}\,\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,\sup_{|\theta|<cn^{-1/2}}\,n\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\big|T(X)-\psi(P_{\theta})\big|^{2}\geq C\left(\int\dot{\psi}_{0}\,g\,dP_{0}\right)^{2}/\left(\int g^{2}\,dP_{0}\right)

where
C\approx 0.28953
. Using Lemma 2.2 of
Van der Vaart, (2002)
and taking the supremum over the linear closure of the tangent set, we conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{g\in\mathcal{T}_{P_{0}}}\,\liminf_{c\longrightarrow\infty}\,\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,\sup_{|\theta|<cn^{-1/2}}\,n\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\big|T(X)-\psi(P_{\theta})\big|^{2}\geq C\int\dot{\psi}^{2}_{0}\,dP_{0}

where
C\approx 0.28953
.
∎

D.3
Proof of Lemma
10

To begin, we define a diffeomorphism
\varphi_{0}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\mapsto B([0],1)
between
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and an open unit ball in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We then construct the following mapping:

\displaystyle\varphi(s):=\theta_{0}+cn^{-1/\alpha}\varphi_{0}(s)

(29)

for all
s\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. The resulting mapping is a valid diffeomorphism between
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
B(\theta_{0},cn^{-1/\alpha})
. Additionally by the differentiablity of
\varphi_{0}
, we have
\nabla\varphi(s)=cn^{-1/\alpha}\nabla\varphi_{0}(s)
. Similar to several preceding proofs such as the proof of Proposition
3
, we apply Theorem
7
to the composition function
t\mapsto\widetilde{\psi}(t):=(\psi\circ\varphi)(t)
and the statistical models

\displaystyle d\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}^{n}_{0}(x,t):=dP^{n}_{\varphi(t)}(x)\,dQ(t)\hskip 9.24994pt\mbox{and}\hskip 9.24994ptd\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}^{n}_{h}(x,t):=dP^{n}_{\varphi(t+h)}(x)\,dQ(t+h),

which implies

\displaystyle\inf_{T}\,\sup_{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\,\mathbb{E}_{\theta}|T(X)-\widetilde{\psi}(\theta)|^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\geq\bigg[\frac{\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\widetilde{\psi}(t)-\widetilde{\psi}(t-h)\right)\,dQ(t)\right|}{2H(\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{0},\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h})}-\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\widetilde{\psi}(t)-\widetilde{\psi}(t-h)\right|^{2}\,dQ(t)\right)^{1/2}\bigg]^{2}_{+}.

for any
h\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Let
t_{*}
be a point in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
t_{*}:=t-\lambda h
for some
\lambda\in[0,1]
and
\vartheta_{t}:=\theta_{0}+cn^{-1/\alpha}\varphi_{0}(t)
. Assuming that
n
is large enough such that
\|\theta_{0}-\vartheta_{t}\|<\delta/2
, then by the Hölder-smoothness assumption of
\psi
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\psi}(t)-\widetilde{\psi}(t-h)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\psi(\theta_{0}+cn^{-1/\alpha}\varphi_{0}(t))-\psi(\theta_{0}+cn^{-1/\alpha}\varphi_{0}(t-h))

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\psi(\vartheta_{t})-\psi(\vartheta_{t}-cn^{-1/\alpha}(\varphi_{0}(t)-\varphi_{0}(t-h)))

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=C_{2}\big(\vartheta_{t},\mathrm{sign}(\varphi_{0}(t)-\varphi_{0}(t-h))\big)c^{\beta}n^{-\beta/\alpha}\|\varphi_{0}(t)-\varphi_{0}(t-h)\|^{\beta}+o(c^{\beta}n^{-\beta/\alpha}\|\varphi_{0}(t)-\varphi_{0}(t-h)\|^{\beta})

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=C_{2}\big(\vartheta_{t},\mathrm{sign}(\varphi_{0}(t)-\varphi_{0}(t-h))\big)c^{\beta}n^{-\beta/\alpha}\|\nabla\varphi_{0}(t_{*})h\|^{\beta}+o(c^{\beta}n^{-\beta/\alpha}\|\nabla\varphi_{0}(t_{*})h\|^{\beta}).

Multiplying by
n^{\beta/\alpha}
and taking the limit as
n\longrightarrow\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,n^{\beta/\alpha}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\widetilde{\psi}(t)-\widetilde{\psi}(t-h)\right)\,dQ(t)\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt~\geq~c^{\beta}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}C_{2}\big(\vartheta_{t},\mathrm{sign}(\varphi_{0}(t)-\varphi_{0}(t-h))\big)\|\nabla\varphi_{0}(t-\lambda h)h\|^{\beta}\,dQ(t)

by Fatou’s lemma. We also have that

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\widetilde{\psi}(t)-\widetilde{\psi}(t-h)\right|^{2}\,dQ(t)\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=c^{\beta}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}C_{2}^{2}\big(\vartheta_{t},\mathrm{sign}(\varphi_{0}(t)-\varphi_{0}(t-h))\big)\|\nabla\varphi_{0}(t-\lambda h)h\|^{2\beta}\,dQ(t)\right)^{1/2}.

by the dominated convergence theorem, which follows since
\nabla\varphi_{0}
is continuous and thus bounded as
h\longrightarrow 0
. Moving onto the Hellinger distance, by the Hölder-smoothness assumption, we have

\displaystyle H^{2}\left(P_{\varphi(t+h)},P_{\varphi(t)}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=H^{2}\left(P_{\theta_{0}+cn^{-1/\alpha}\varphi_{0}(t+h)},P_{\theta_{0}+cn^{-1/\alpha}\varphi_{0}(t)}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=H^{2}\left(P_{\theta_{t}+cn^{-1/\alpha}(\varphi_{0}(t+h)-\varphi_{0}(t))},P_{\theta_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=C_{1}\big(\vartheta_{t},\mathrm{sign}(\varphi_{0}(t+h)-\varphi_{0}(t))\big)c^{\alpha}n^{-1}\|\varphi_{0}(t+h)-\varphi_{0}(t)\|^{\alpha}+o(c^{\alpha}n^{-1}\|\varphi_{0}(t+h)-\varphi_{0}(t)\|^{\alpha})

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=C_{1}\big(\vartheta_{t},\mathrm{sign}(\varphi_{0}(t+h)-\varphi_{0}(t))\big)c^{\alpha}n^{-1}\|\nabla\varphi_{0}(t+\lambda h)h\|^{\alpha}+o(c^{\alpha}n^{-1}\|\nabla\varphi_{0}(t+\lambda h)h\|^{\alpha}),

which then followed by the tensorization property of the Hellinger distance to yield

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,H^{2}\left(P^{n}_{\varphi(t+h)},P^{n}_{\varphi(t)}\right)

\displaystyle=\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,\left\{2-2\left(1-\frac{H^{2}\left(P_{\theta_{0}+cn^{-1/\alpha}\varphi_{0}(t)},P_{\theta_{0}+cn^{-1/\alpha}\varphi_{0}(t-h)}\right)}{2}\right)^{n}\right\}

\displaystyle=2-2\exp\left(-\frac{C_{1}\big(\vartheta_{t},\mathrm{sign}(\varphi_{0}(t+h)-\varphi_{0}(t))\big)c^{\alpha}\|\nabla\varphi_{0}(t+\lambda h)h\|^{\alpha}}{2}\right).

We then conclude that

\displaystyle H^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{0},\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h})

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=H^{2}(Q_{h},Q_{0})+\int_{\mathbb{R}}H^{2}(P^{n}_{\varphi(t+h)},P^{n}_{\varphi(t)})\,dQ_{h}^{1/2}\,dQ^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=H^{2}(Q_{h},Q_{0})+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left\{2-2\exp\left(-\frac{C_{1}\big(\vartheta_{t},\mathrm{sign}(\varphi_{0}(t+h)-\varphi_{0}(t))\big)c^{\alpha}\|\nabla\varphi_{0}(t+\lambda h)h\|^{\alpha}}{2}\right)\right\}\,dQ_{h}^{1/2}\,dQ^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=2-2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\exp\left(-\frac{C_{1}\big(\vartheta_{t},\mathrm{sign}(\varphi_{0}(t+h)-\varphi_{0}(t))\big)c^{\alpha}\|\nabla\varphi_{0}(t+\lambda h)h\|^{\alpha}}{2}\right)\,dQ_{h}^{1/2}\,dQ^{1/2}.

For the ease of notation, we denote the constants as follows:

\widetilde{C}_{1}(t):=C_{1}\big(\vartheta_{t},\mathrm{sign}(\varphi_{0}(t+h)-\varphi_{0}(t))\big)\hskip 9.24994pt\mbox{and}\hskip 9.24994pt\widetilde{C}_{2}(t):=C_{2}\big(\vartheta_{t},\mathrm{sign}(\varphi_{0}(t)-\varphi_{0}(t-h))\big).

Then, putting these intermediate results together, we have

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,\inf_{T}\,\sup_{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\,n^{2\beta/\alpha}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}|T(X)-\widetilde{\psi}(\theta)|^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\geq\left[\frac{c^{\beta}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\widetilde{C}_{2}(t)\|\nabla\varphi_{0}(t-\lambda h)h\|^{\beta}\,dQ(t)}{2\left\{2-2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\exp\left(-\widetilde{C}_{1}(t)c^{\alpha}\|\nabla\varphi_{0}(t+\lambda h)h\|^{\alpha}/2\right)\,dQ_{h}^{1/2}\,dQ^{1/2}\right\}^{1/2}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt-c^{\beta}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\widetilde{C}^{2}_{2}(t)\|\nabla\varphi_{0}(t-\lambda h)h\|^{2\beta}\,dQ(t)\right)^{1/2}\bigg]^{2}_{+}

where the above display holds for any
h\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We denote
\bar{h}=hc
and let
c\longrightarrow\infty
. This further simplifies the expression to

\displaystyle\liminf_{c\longrightarrow\infty}\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,\inf_{T}\,\sup_{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\,n^{2\beta/\alpha}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}|T(X)-\widetilde{\psi}(\theta)|^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\geq\liminf_{\bar{h}\longrightarrow\infty}\left[\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\widetilde{C}_{2}(t)\|\nabla\varphi_{0}(t)\bar{h}\|^{\beta}\,dQ(t)}{2\left\{2-2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\exp\left(-\widetilde{C}_{1}(t)\|\nabla\varphi_{0}(t)\bar{h}\|^{\alpha}/2\right)\,dQ\right\}^{1/2}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt-\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\widetilde{C}^{2}_{2}(t)\|\nabla\varphi_{0}(t)\bar{h}\|^{2\beta}\,dQ(t)\right)^{1/2}\bigg]^{2}_{+}.

We claim that there exists the choice of
\varphi_{0}
and
Q
to make the lower bound strictly greater than zero. Since the above inequality holds for any
\varphi_{0}
and
\bar{h}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we consider the class of diffeomorphism such that

\|\nabla\varphi_{0}\bar{h}\|\longrightarrow\gamma\hskip 9.24994pt\text{as}\hskip 9.24994pt\|\bar{h}\|\longrightarrow\infty.

Denoting
\bar{h}=u\varepsilon
for
u\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, this can be obtained, for instance, by

\varphi_{0}(t)=\frac{2}{\pi}\arctan\left(\frac{\pi\gamma\|t\|}{2\varepsilon}\right)\hskip 9.24994pt\mbox{and}\hskip 9.24994pt\nabla\varphi_{0}(t)=\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon}\left\{1+\left(\frac{\pi\gamma\|t\|}{2\varepsilon}\right)^{2}\right\}^{-1}\frac{t}{\|t\|}.

For any choice of
\gamma>0
, the image of
\varphi_{0}
is
B([0],1)
and
\|\nabla\varphi_{0}\bar{h}\|\longrightarrow\gamma
as
\|\bar{h}\|\longrightarrow\infty
. Since
C_{2}(t,\mathrm{sign}(h))\leq\overline{C}_{1}
and
C_{2}(t,\mathrm{sign}(h))\leq\overline{C}_{2}
uniformly, we obtain

\displaystyle\liminf_{c\longrightarrow\infty}\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,\inf_{T}\,\sup_{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\,n^{2\beta/\alpha}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}|T(X)-\widetilde{\psi}(\theta)|^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\geq\left[\frac{\gamma^{\beta}\int\widetilde{C}_{2}(t)\,dQ}{2\left\{2-2\exp\left(-\overline{C}_{1}\gamma^{\alpha}/2\right)\right\}^{1/2}}-\gamma^{\beta}\left(\int\widetilde{C}^{2}_{2}(t)\,dQ\right)^{1/2}\right]^{2}_{+}.

Choosing
\gamma=(2\Delta/\overline{C}_{1})^{1/\alpha}
for some
\Delta>0
, the lower bound becomes

\displaystyle\left[\frac{(2\Delta/\overline{C}_{1})^{\beta/\alpha}\int\widetilde{C}_{2}(t)\,dQ}{2\left\{2-2\exp\left(-\Delta\right)\right\}^{1/2}}-(2\Delta/\overline{C}_{1})^{\beta/\alpha}\left(\int\widetilde{C}^{2}_{2}(t)\,dQ\right)^{1/2}\right]^{2}_{+}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=(2\Delta/\overline{C}_{1})^{2\beta/\alpha}\left[\frac{\int\widetilde{C}_{2}(t)\,dQ}{2\left\{2-2\exp\left(-\Delta\right)\right\}^{1/2}}-\left(\int\widetilde{C}^{2}_{2}(t)\,dQ\right)^{1/2}\right]_{+}^{2}.

The final display is strictly greater than zero when

\displaystyle\frac{\int\widetilde{C}_{2}(t)\,dQ}{2\left\{2-2\exp\left(-\delta\right)\right\}^{1/2}}-\left(\int\widetilde{C}^{2}_{2}(t)\,dQ\right)^{1/2}>0\Longrightarrow\frac{\int\widetilde{C}_{2}(t)\,dQ}{\left(\int\widetilde{C}^{2}_{2}(t)\,dQ\right)^{1/2}}>2\left\{2-2\exp\left(-\Delta\right)\right\}^{1/2}.

Hence, it suffices to show that there exists
Q
such that the

\int\widetilde{C}_{2}(t)\,dQ/\left(\int\widetilde{C}^{2}_{2}(t)\,dQ\right)^{1/2}>\varepsilon

satisfies for any
\varepsilon>0
. By the assumption that
\overline{C}_{2}
is strictly positive and it is continuous in
t
around
\overline{C}_{2}
, we have
\int\widetilde{C}_{2}(t)\,dQ>\underline{c}_{2}
and
\int\widetilde{C}^{2}_{2}(t)\,dQ\leq\|C^{2}_{2}\|_{\infty}<\infty
by the Hölder inequality. Finally, selecting
\Delta
small enough such that

\displaystyle\underline{c}_{2}/\overline{C}_{2}^{2}>2\left\{2-2\exp\left(-\Delta\right)\right\}^{1/2},

the lower bound becomes strictly positive.

Appendix E
Proof of Lemma
11

Let
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
be drawn IID from the unknown density
f_{0}
. In this section, we develop a local asymptotic minimax lower bound for the density at
X=x_{0}
, which is a non-differentiable functional. We consider the approximation functional via convolution such that

\displaystyle\psi(f):=f(x_{0})\hskip 9.24994pt\textrm{and}\hskip 9.24994pt\phi(f):=\int h^{-1}K\left(\frac{x-x_{0}}{h}\right)\,f(x)\,dx

where
K
is a function, satisfying
(A1)
, and
h>0
is a bandwidth parameter. We assume that
f_{0}
is
s
-times continuously differentiable at
x_{0}
. We consider the following set of density functions:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(s,x_{0},M):=\left\{f\textrm{ is $s$-times differentiable at $x_{0}$, satisfying }|f^{(s)}(x_{0})|\leq(1+M)|f^{(s)}_{0}(x_{0})|\right\}

Furthermore, we define the following localized set of density functions

\displaystyle U(\delta;\varepsilon):=\left\{f\in\mathcal{F}(s,x_{0},M)\,:\,\int_{|x-x_{0}|\leq\varepsilon}\,|f^{(k)}(x)-f^{(k)}_{0}(x)|\,dx\leq\delta\hskip 9.24994pt\textrm{for all }k\in\{0,1,\ldots,s\}\right\}.

Proof of Lemma
11
.

Throughout the proof,
\varepsilon
and
M
are considered fixed. With the choice of a kernel function, satisfying
(A1)
, the approximation functional
\phi
is absolutely continuous with respect to
f
and thus Theorem
5
applies. It then implies for any approximating functional
\phi
,

\displaystyle\inf_{T}\,\sup_{f\in U(\delta;\varepsilon)}\,\mathbb{E}_{f}|T(X)-\psi(f)|^{2}\geq\sup_{Q}\,\left[\sqrt{\frac{|\int_{\Theta}\phi^{\prime}(f)\,dQ|^{2}}{\mathcal{I}(Q)+\int_{\Theta}\mathcal{I}(f)\,dQ}}-\|\psi-\phi\|_{L_{2}(Q)}\right]_{+}^{2}.

(30)

where the probability measure
Q
is placed over the collection of
f
, which we formalize by constructing a differentiable parametric submodel. Since the current result holds for any choice of
\phi
, and thus for any
h>0
, the lower bound still holds by restricting ourselves to the choice of
h\in(0,\varepsilon\wedge 1)
. Similarly, the lower bound is agnostic to the choice of
K
. Thus we focus on the
K
whose support is contained in
[-1,1]
. Throughout this section, we denote by
\mathfrak{C}
a fixed constant that may vary line by line.

Local parameter space

For any function
g\in L_{0}^{2}(f_{0})
, that is, a mean-zero and finite variance under the density
f_{0}
, we define a differentiable parametric path of densities as

\displaystyle f_{t}(x)=(1+tg(x))f_{0}(x)

for all
x\in\mathcal{X}
and
t\geq 0
. As Theorem
5
holds for any choice of differentiable paths, we consider the choice of
g=\Phi_{0}
such that

\displaystyle\Phi_{0}:=x\mapsto h^{-1}K\left(\frac{x-x_{0}}{h}\right)-\int h^{-1}K\left(\frac{x-x_{0}}{h}\right)f_{0}(x)\,dx.

It is straightforward to verify
\Phi_{0}
is a valid score function. In fact,
\Phi_{0}
is an efficient influence function of the functional
\mathbb{E}_{f}[\phi]
relative to the maximal tangent space. We then consider the particular choice of paths given by
\widetilde{f}_{t}(x):=(1+t\Phi_{0}(x))f_{0}(x)
.

First, we ensure the choice of parameter permits
\widetilde{f}_{t}
to belong to the class of interest for all
t
. The score function
\Phi_{0}
is bounded uniformly as

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{0}\|_{\infty}\leq\left\|h^{-1}K\left(\frac{x-x_{0}}{h}\right)-\int h^{-1}K\left(\frac{x-x_{0}}{h}\right)f_{0}(x)\,dx\right\|_{\infty}\leq 2h^{-1}\|K\|_{\infty}.

Since
K
is uniformly bounded, it follows that

\displaystyle 1+t\Phi_{0}(x)\geq 1-t\|\Phi_{0}\|_{\infty}\geq 1-2th^{-1}\|K\|_{\infty}.

Therefore the induced path generates a nonnegative function (that integrates to one) so long as
t\leq h/(2\|K\|_{\infty})
. Next, we examine the smoothness of the induced path. Since
\Phi_{0}^{(k)}(x)=h^{-(k+1)}K^{(k)}\left((x-x_{0})/h\right)
, it follows

\displaystyle|\widetilde{f}_{t}^{(s)}(x_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq|f_{0}^{(s)}(x_{0})|+\left|\sum_{k=0}^{s}{s\choose k}t\left(\frac{d^{s-k}}{dx^{s-k}}\Phi_{0}(x_{0})\right)f_{0}^{(k)}(x_{0})\right|

\displaystyle\leq|f_{0}^{(s)}(x_{0})|+\sum_{k=0}^{s}{s\choose k}\left(\frac{t}{h^{s-k+1}}|K^{(s-k)}(0)|\right)|f_{0}^{(k)}(x_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq|f_{0}^{(s)}(x_{0})|+t\left\{\frac{|K^{(s)}(0)|}{h^{s+1}}f_{0}(x_{0})+\frac{\mathfrak{C}}{h^{s}}\right\}

for some constant
\mathfrak{C}>0
. We then define

\displaystyle C_{M}:=\left(\frac{1-h\mathfrak{C}}{|K^{(s)}(0)|f_{0}(x_{0})}\right)M|f_{0}^{(s)}(x_{0})|,

for
t\in[0,C_{M}h^{s+1}]
. With this choice of
t
, the density on the path satisfies
|\widetilde{f}_{t}^{(s)}(x_{0})|\leq(1+M)|f_{0}^{(s)}(x_{0})|
. Next, we observe that for any
\widetilde{f}_{t}

\displaystyle\int_{|x-x_{0}|\leq\varepsilon}|\widetilde{f}^{(\ell)}_{t}(x)-f^{(\ell)}_{0}(x)|\,dx

\displaystyle=t\sum_{k=0}^{\ell}{\ell\choose k}\frac{1}{h^{\ell-k+1}}\int_{|x-x_{0}|\leq\varepsilon}\left|K^{(\ell-k)}\left(\frac{x-x_{0}}{h}\right)f_{0}^{(k)}(x)\right|\,dx

\displaystyle\leq th^{-\ell}\sup_{|x-x_{0}|\leq h}\,|f_{0}(x)|\int_{-1}^{1}|K^{(\ell)}(u)|\,du+\mathfrak{C}h^{-(\ell-1)}

where the last step follows since
h<(1\wedge\varepsilon)
. All densities on the parametric path hence belong to
U(\delta;\varepsilon)
so long as

\displaystyle t\leq\frac{\delta h^{s}}{\sup_{|x-x_{0}|\leq h}\,|f_{0}(x)|\int_{-1}^{1}|K^{(s)}(u)|\,du+\mathfrak{C}h}=\delta h^{s}\Delta\hskip 9.24994pt\textrm{where}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\Delta:=\left(\sup_{|x-x_{0}|\leq h}\,|f_{0}(x)|\int_{-1}^{1}|K^{(s)}(u)|\,du+\mathfrak{C}h\right)^{-1}.

It remains to analyze the lower bound given by (
30
) over the parametric submodel
\{\widetilde{f}_{t}:t\in\Theta_{0}\}
where

\displaystyle\Theta_{0}:=\left\{t:0\leq t\leq(\delta h^{s}\Delta\wedge C_{M}h^{s+1})\right\}.

This suggests that we need to analyze two regimes,
\delta\lesssim h
and
h\lesssim\delta
. For instance, the usual global minimax lower bound over
\mathcal{F}
corresponds to taking
\delta
as a fixed constant.

Approximation bias

Let
\mathfrak{B}:=(\delta h^{s}\Delta\wedge C_{M}h^{s+1})
. Based on the earlier derivation, we consider a prior Q supported on
[0,\mathfrak{B}]
. Such a prior can be constructed from the following dilation:

\displaystyle Q(t):=\frac{2}{\mathfrak{B}}Q_{0}\left(\frac{t-\mathfrak{B}/2}{\mathfrak{B}/2}\right)\hskip 9.24994pt\textrm{where}\hskip 9.24994ptQ_{0}(t):=\cos^{2}(\pi t/2)I(|t|\leq 1).

The choice of the cosine density is motivated by the fact that this density minimizes Fisher information among density with support over
[-1,1]

(
Uhrmann-Klingen,, 1995
)
. The corresponding Fisher information is
\mathcal{I}(Q_{0})=\pi^{2}
. First, for any
\widetilde{f}_{t}
over
t\in\Theta_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\psi-\phi\|_{L_{2}(Q)}

\displaystyle=\left(\int|\psi(\widetilde{f}_{t})-\phi(\widetilde{f}_{t})|^{2}\,dQ(t)\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\left(\int\left|\widetilde{f}_{t}(x_{0})-\int h^{-1}K\left(\frac{x-x_{0}}{h}\right)\,\widetilde{f}_{t}(x)\,dx\right|^{2}\,dQ(t)\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\left(\int\left|\int_{-1}^{1}K(u)\int_{x_{0}}^{x_{0}+uh}\frac{\widetilde{f}_{t}^{(s)}(\xi)(x_{0}+uh-\xi)^{s-1}}{(s-1)!}\,d\xi\,du\right|^{2}\,dQ(t)\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int\left|\int_{-1}^{1}K(u)\frac{|uh|^{s-1}}{(s-1)!}\int_{x_{0}}^{x_{0}+uh}|\widetilde{f}_{t}^{(s)}(\xi)|\,d\xi\,du\right|^{2}\,dQ(t)\right)^{1/2}

Furthermore, it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{x_{0}}^{x_{0}+uh}|\widetilde{f}_{t}^{(s)}(\xi)|\,d\xi

\displaystyle=\int_{x_{0}}^{x_{0}+uh}\left|f_{0}^{(s)}(\xi)+\sum_{k=0}^{s}{s\choose k}\left(\frac{t}{h^{s-k+1}}K^{(s-k)}((\xi-x_{0})/h)\right)\,f_{0}^{(k)}(\xi)\right|\,d\xi

\displaystyle\leq\int_{x_{0}}^{x_{0}+uh}|f_{0}^{(s)}(\xi)|\,d\xi+\int_{x_{0}}^{x_{0}+uh}\left|\frac{t}{h^{s+1}}K^{(s)}((\xi-x_{0})/h)\,f_{0}(\xi)+\mathfrak{C}h^{-s}\right|\,d\xi

\displaystyle\leq\int_{x_{0}}^{x_{0}+uh}|f_{0}^{(s)}(\xi)|\,d\xi+\frac{t}{h^{s}}\int_{0}^{u}\left|K^{(s)}(\zeta)\,f_{0}(x_{0}+\zeta h)+\mathfrak{C}h\right|\,d\zeta

\displaystyle\leq|uh|\sup_{|x-x_{0}|\leq h}|f_{0}^{(s)}(x)|+\frac{\mathfrak{B}}{h^{s}}\sup_{|x-x_{0}|\leq h}|f_{0}(x)|\int_{0}^{u}|K^{(s)}(\zeta)+\mathfrak{C}h|\,d\zeta.

The lower order terms cancel by the fact that
\int u^{k}K(u)\,du=0
for all
k\in\{1,2,\ldots,s-1\}
. Also by the definition of
\mathfrak{B}
, it follows
\mathfrak{B}/h^{s}=(\delta\Delta\wedge C_{M}h)\leq C_{M}h
. This leads to

\displaystyle\left(\int\left|\int_{-1}^{1}K(u)\frac{|uh|^{s-1}}{(s-1)!}\int_{x_{0}}^{x_{0}+uh}|\widetilde{f}_{t}^{(s)}(\xi)|\,d\xi\,du\right|^{2}\,dQ(t)\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\leq\left|\int_{-1}^{1}K(u)\frac{|uh|^{s-1}}{(s-1)!}\left(|uh|\sup_{|x-x_{0}|\leq h}|f_{0}^{(s)}(x)|+C_{M}h\sup_{|x-x_{0}|\leq h}|f_{0}(x)|\int_{0}^{u}|K^{(s)}(\zeta)+\mathfrak{C}h|\,d\zeta\right)\,du\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\leq h^{s}\left|\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{K(u)|u|^{s}}{(s-1)!}\,du\left(\sup_{|x-x_{0}|\leq h}|f_{0}^{(s)}(x)|+C_{M}\int_{-1}^{1}|K^{(s)}(\zeta)|\,d\zeta\sup_{|x-x_{0}|\leq h}|f_{0}(x)|+\mathfrak{C}h\right)\right|

Thus, we conclude that

\displaystyle\|\psi-\phi\|_{L_{2}(Q)}\leq Bh^{s}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\textrm{where}\hskip 9.24994ptB:=\left|\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{K(u)|u|^{s}}{(s-1)!}\,du\left(\sup_{|x-x_{0}|\leq h}|f_{0}^{(s)}(x)|+\frac{C_{M}}{\Delta}+\mathfrak{C}h\right)\right|

(31)

and this term no longer depends on
Q
.

Surrogate efficiency

We now turn to the numerator and the denominator from the first term of (
30
). First, by the choice of differentiable paths, the derivative with respect to
t
is given by
\Phi_{0}(x)f_{0}(x)
. This does not depend on
t
as we constructed a linear path. Hence the numerator of (
30
) is given by

\displaystyle\sqrt{\left|\int_{0\leq t<\mathfrak{B}}\phi^{\prime}(\widetilde{f}_{t})\,dQ\right|^{2}}=\sqrt{\left|\int\frac{1}{h}K\left(\frac{x-x_{0}}{h}\right)\Phi_{0}(x)f_{0}(x)\,dx\right|^{2}}.

This result also comes directly from the fact that the approximation functional
\phi
is pathwise differentiable with its efficient influence function given by
\Phi_{0}
. We can further write out this term as

\displaystyle\int\frac{1}{h}K\left(\frac{x-x_{0}}{h}\right)\Phi_{0}(x)f_{0}(x)\,dx

\displaystyle=\int\frac{1}{h^{2}}K^{2}\left(\frac{x-x_{0}}{h}\right)f_{0}(x)\,dx-\left(\int h^{-1}K\left(\frac{x-x_{0}}{h}\right)f_{0}(x)\,dx\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=h^{-1}\int_{-1}^{1}K^{2}(u)f_{0}(x_{0}+uh)\,du-\left(\int_{-1}^{1}K(u)f_{0}(x_{0}+uh)\,du\right)^{2}.

We introduce the notation

\displaystyle V_{1}:=\left|\int_{-1}^{1}K^{2}(u)f_{0}(x_{0}+uh)\,du-h\left(\int_{-1}^{1}K(u)f_{0}(x_{0}+uh)\,du\right)^{2}\right|,

(32)

and the numerator can be denoted by
h^{-1}V_{1}
. We now move onto the denominator. First, the Fisher information of the dilation
Q
is simply given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(Q)=\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\mathfrak{B}^{2}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textrm{where}\hskip 9.24994pt\mathfrak{B}:=(\delta h^{s}\Delta\wedge C_{M}h^{s+1}).

(33)

The Fisher information associated with
\widetilde{f}_{t}
under the
n
IID observations is given by

\displaystyle\int\left(\frac{\frac{d}{dt}\widetilde{f}_{t}^{n}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})}{\widetilde{f}_{t}^{n}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})}\right)^{2}\widetilde{f}_{t}^{n}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\,d(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\int\left(\frac{\widetilde{f}_{t}^{\prime}(x_{1})\widetilde{f}_{t}(x_{2},\ldots,x_{n})+\ldots+\widetilde{f}_{t}^{\prime}(x_{n})\widetilde{f}_{t}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n-1})}{\widetilde{f}_{t}^{n}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})}\right)^{2}\widetilde{f}_{t}^{n}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\,d(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\int\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\widetilde{f}_{t}^{\prime}(x_{i})}{\widetilde{f}_{t}(x_{i})}\right)^{2}\widetilde{f}_{t}^{n}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\,d(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=n\int\left(\frac{\widetilde{f}_{t}^{\prime}(x)}{\widetilde{f}_{t}(x)}\right)^{2}\widetilde{f}_{t}(x)\,dx+\sum_{i\neq j}\int\widetilde{f}_{t}^{\prime}(x_{i})\,dx_{i}\int\widetilde{f}_{t}^{\prime}(x_{j})\,dx_{j}

Since the choice of the path implies
\widetilde{f}_{t}^{\prime}=\Phi_{0}(x)f_{0}(x)
, which integrates to zero, the Fisher information for the path for each
t\in\Theta_{0}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(t)=n\int\frac{\Phi_{0}^{2}(x)f^{2}_{0}(x)}{(1-t\Phi_{0}(x))f_{0}(x)}\,dx=n\int\frac{\Phi_{0}^{2}(x)f_{0}(x)}{(1-t\Phi_{0}(x))}\,dx.

The denominator of (
30
) is thus given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(Q)+n\int_{\Theta_{0}}\mathcal{I}(\widetilde{f}_{t})\,dQ(t)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\mathfrak{B}^{2}}+n\int_{0\leq t\leq\mathfrak{B}}\int\frac{\Phi^{2}_{0}(x)f_{0}(x)}{(1-t\Phi(x))}\,dx\,dQ(t)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\mathfrak{B}^{2}}+n\int_{-1}^{1}\int\frac{\Phi^{2}_{0}(x)f_{0}(x)}{(1-(\mathfrak{B}/2+t\mathfrak{B}/2)\Phi_{0}(x))}\,dx\,dQ_{0}(t).

As it follows that

\displaystyle\int\frac{\Phi^{2}_{0}(x)f_{0}(x)}{(1-(\mathfrak{B}/2+t\mathfrak{B}/2)\Phi_{0}(x))}\,dx

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\leq\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{h^{-1}K^{2}(u)f_{0}(x_{0}+uh)\,}{(1-(\mathfrak{B}/2+t\mathfrak{B}/2)\{K(u)f_{0}(x_{0}+uh)-\int K(u^{\prime})f_{0}(x_{0}+u^{\prime}h)\,du^{\prime}\})}\,du

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\leq\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{h^{-1}K^{2}(u)f_{0}(x_{0}+uh)\,}{(1-(\mathfrak{B}/2+t\mathfrak{B}/2)K(u)f_{0}(x_{0}+uh))}\,du,

we conclude

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(Q)+n\int_{\Theta_{0}}\mathcal{I}(\widetilde{f}_{t})\,dQ(t)\leq\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\mathfrak{B}^{2}}+nh^{-1}V_{2}

where

\displaystyle V_{2}:=\int_{-1}^{1}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{K^{2}(u)f_{0}(x_{0}+uh)\,}{(1-(\mathfrak{B}/2+t\mathfrak{B}/2)K(u)f_{0}(x_{0}+uh))}\,du\,dQ_{0}(t).

(34)

Optimization

Putting together all intermediate results (
31
), (
32
), (
33
), and (
34
) together and plugging them into (
30
), we obtain

\displaystyle\inf_{T}\,\sup_{f\in U(\delta)}\,\mathbb{E}_{f}|T(X)-\psi(f)|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\left[\sqrt{\frac{h^{-2}V_{1}^{2}}{\mathfrak{B}^{-2}4\pi^{2}+nh^{-1}V_{2}}}-\sqrt{h^{2s}B^{2}}\right]_{+}^{2}.

Now we consider two cases.

When
\mathfrak{B}=C_{M}h^{s+1}
.

In this case, the lower bound becomes

\displaystyle\left[\sqrt{\frac{V_{1}^{2}}{C_{M}^{-2}h^{-2s}4\pi^{2}+nhV_{2}}}-\sqrt{h^{2s}B^{2}}\right]_{+}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\left[\sqrt{\frac{V_{1}^{2}h^{2s}}{C_{M}^{-2}4\pi^{2}+nh^{2s+1}V_{2}}}-\sqrt{h^{2s}B^{2}}\right]_{+}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\frac{h^{2s}}{C_{M}^{-2}4\pi^{2}+nh^{2s+1}V_{2}}\left[\sqrt{V_{1}^{2}}-\sqrt{B^{2}(C_{M}^{-2}4\pi^{2}+nh^{2s+1}V_{2})}\right]_{+}^{2}.

Recall that the present lower bound holds for any
h
, we choose an optimal choice of
h
for the term outside of the bracket. The optimal choice here is given by

\displaystyle h=\left(\frac{8sC_{M}^{-2}\pi^{2}}{nV_{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2s+1}},

and the corresponding lower bound is

\displaystyle\inf_{T}\sup_{f\in U(\delta)}\mathbb{E}_{f}|T(X)-f(x_{0})|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{8s^{2s/(2s+1)}}{(4+8s)}\left(C_{M}^{-2}\pi^{2}\right)^{\frac{-1}{2s+1}}\left(\frac{1}{nV_{2}}\right)^{\frac{2s}{2s+1}}\left[V_{1}-BC_{M}^{-1}\pi\sqrt{(4+8s)}\right]_{+}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{8s^{2s/(2s+1)}}{(4+8s)}\pi^{-\frac{2}{2s+1}}C_{M}^{-\frac{4s}{2s+1}}\left(\frac{1}{nV_{2}}\right)^{\frac{2s}{2s+1}}\left[V_{1}C_{M}-B\pi\sqrt{(4+8s)}\right]_{+}^{2}.

The above inequality holds for any
n\geq 1
. To derive a simpler asymptotic constant, we multiply both sides on the inequality by
n^{2s/(2s+1)}
and consider the limit as
n\longrightarrow\infty
. First, we recall from the definition
\mathfrak{B}\longrightarrow 0
as
n\longrightarrow\infty
and thus

\displaystyle V_{1},V_{2}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\int K^{2}(u)\,duf_{0}(x_{0})\hskip 9.24994pt\textrm{,}\hskip 9.24994ptC_{M}\longrightarrow\left(\frac{M|f_{0}^{(s)}(x_{0})|}{|K^{(s)}(0)|f_{0}(x_{0})}\right)\hskip 9.24994pt\textrm{and}

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle\longrightarrow|f_{0}^{(s)}(x_{0})|\left|\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{K(u)|u|^{s}}{(s-1)!}\,du\left(1+\frac{M\int_{-1}^{1}|K^{(s)}(u)|\,du}{|K^{(s)}(0)|}\right)\right|.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,\inf_{T}\sup_{f\in U(\delta)}n^{2s/(2s+1)}\mathbb{E}_{f}|T(X)-f(x_{0})|^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\geq C(s)f_{0}(x_{0})^{2s/(2s+1)}|f_{0}^{(s)}(x_{0})|^{2/(2s+1)}\left(\frac{|K^{(s)}(0)|}{M}\right)^{\frac{4s}{2s+1}}\left(\int K^{2}(u)\,du\right)^{2s/(2s+1)}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\left[\frac{M\int K^{2}(u)\,du}{|K^{(s)}(0)|}-\sqrt{\pi^{2}(4+8s)}\left|\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{K(u)|u|^{s}}{(s-1)!}\,du\left(1+\frac{M\int_{-1}^{1}|K^{(s)}(u)|\,du}{|K^{(s)}(0)|}\right)\right|\right]_{+}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=C(s,M,K)f_{0}(x_{0})^{2s/(2s+1)}|f_{0}^{(s)}(x_{0})|^{2/(2s+1)}

where
C(s)
is a constant only depending on
s
and

\displaystyle C(s,M,K)

\displaystyle:=\frac{8s^{2s/(2s+1)}}{(4+8s)}\pi^{-\frac{2}{2s+1}}\left(\frac{|K^{(s)}(0)|}{M}\right)^{\frac{4s}{2s+1}}\left(\int K^{2}(u)\,du\right)^{2s/(2s+1)}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\left[\frac{M\int K^{2}(u)\,du}{|K^{(s)}(0)|}-\sqrt{\pi^{2}(4+8s)}\left|\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{K(u)|u|^{s}}{(s-1)!}\,du\left(1+\frac{M\int_{-1}^{1}|K^{(s)}(u)|\,du}{|K^{(s)}(0)|}\right)\right|\right]_{+}^{2}.

Crucially, the expression above does not depend on
f_{0}
. Finally, we can take
\mathfrak{B}=C_{M}h^{s+1}
when
\Delta\delta/C_{M}>h
, meaning that

\displaystyle\delta>\frac{C_{M}}{\Delta}\left(\frac{8s\pi^{2}}{C_{M}^{2}V_{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2s+1}}n^{-\frac{1}{2s+1}}

This is certainly the case when
\delta=c_{0}n^{-r}
for any
r<\frac{1}{2s+1}
. This proves the result.
∎

We provide the corresponding results outside of the setting of Lemma
11
. First, when
r=\frac{1}{2s+1}
and
c_{0}>\frac{C_{M}}{\Delta}\left(\frac{8s\pi^{2}}{C_{M}^{2}V_{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2s+1}}
, the above bound remains valid. Next, we consider when
\mathfrak{B}=\Delta h^{s}\delta
. This is the case when
\Delta\delta/C_{M}<h
. In this case, we consider the choice
h\downarrow\Delta\delta/C_{M}
. The corresponding lower bound is

\displaystyle\inf_{T}\,\sup_{f\in U(\delta)}\,\mathbb{E}_{f}|T(X)-\psi(f)|^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\geq\left[\sqrt{\frac{V_{1}^{2}}{\Delta^{-2}h^{-2s}(h/\delta)^{2}4\pi^{2}+nhV_{2}}}-\sqrt{h^{2s}B^{2}}\right]_{+}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\geq\left[\sqrt{\frac{V_{1}^{2}(\Delta c_{0}n^{-r}/C_{M})^{2s}}{C_{M}^{-2}4\pi^{2}+n(\Delta c_{0}n^{-r}/C_{M})^{2s+1}V_{2}}}-\sqrt{(\Delta c_{0}n^{-r}/C_{M})^{2s}B^{2}}\right]_{+}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\frac{1}{n^{1-r}}\left[\sqrt{\frac{V_{1}^{2}(\Delta c_{0}n^{-r}/C_{M})^{2s}}{C_{M}^{-2}4\pi^{2}/(n^{1-r})+((\Delta c_{0}/C_{M})(\Delta c_{0}n^{-r}/C_{M})^{2s})V_{2}}}-\sqrt{n^{1-r}(\Delta c_{0}n^{-r}/C_{M})^{2s}B^{2}}\right]_{+}^{2}.

Multiplying both sides of the expression by
n^{1-r}
and taking the limit as
n\longrightarrow\infty
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,\inf_{T}\,\sup_{f\in U(\delta)}\,n^{1-r}\mathbb{E}_{f}|T(X)-\psi(f)|^{2}\geq 0

since the first term in the square bracket converges to a constant while the second term in the square bracket is divergent as
n^{1-r}\longrightarrow\infty
.
We can take
\mathfrak{B}=\Delta h^{s}\delta
when
\Delta\delta/C_{M}<h
, which is the case
\delta=c_{0}n^{-r}
for any
r>\frac{1}{2s+1}
. When
r=\frac{1}{2s+1}
and
c_{0}<\frac{C_{M}}{\Delta}\left(\frac{8s\pi^{2}}{C_{M}^{2}V_{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2s+1}}
, we have

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,\inf_{T}\,\sup_{f\in U(\delta)}\,n^{2s/(2s+1)}\mathbb{E}_{f}|T(X)-\psi(f)|^{2}\geq 0,

Hence this derivation leads to a trivial lower bound when
r\geq 1/(2s+1)
.

Appendix F
Proof of Lemma
12

In this section, we aim to derive a concrete constant for the lower bound given by Lemma
12
in the main text. Suppose
n
IID observation is drawn from
P_{0}
, which belongs to the local model
\{P_{\theta}:|\theta|<\delta\}
for any
\delta>0
. The functional of interest is
\psi(\theta)=\max(0,\theta)
, which is non-smooth at
\theta=0
. We apply Theorem
5
and obtain the lower bound

\displaystyle\inf_{T}\,\sup_{|\theta|<\delta}\,\mathbb{E}_{\theta}|T(X)-\psi(\theta)|^{2}\geq\inf_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\dagger}}\,\frac{\left(\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}I(t>0)q(t)\,dt\right)^{2}}{\mathcal{I}(Q)+n\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}\mathcal{I}(t)q(t)\,dt}

where
\mathcal{Q}^{\dagger}\equiv\mathcal{Q}^{\dagger}(-\delta,\delta)
is the set of “nice” probability measures on
(-\delta,\delta)
that satisfies Definition
1
. We further define the density function
q
as the dilation of the probability density
\nu
such that
q(t):=\delta^{-1}\nu(t/\delta)
for
\delta>0
. The density
\nu
is defined on
[-1,1]
. Then by simple change of variables with
u=t/\delta
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}I(t>0)q(t)\,dt=\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}I(t>0)\delta^{-1}\nu(t/\delta)\,dt=\int_{-1}^{1}I(u>0)\nu(u)\,du,

\displaystyle\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}n\mathcal{I}(t)q(t)\,dt=\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}n\mathcal{I}(t)\delta^{-1}\nu(t/\delta)\,dt=\int_{-1}^{1}n\mathcal{I}(\delta u)\nu(u)\,du

and
\delta^{2}\mathcal{I}(Q)=\mathcal{I}(\nu)
. Plugging them in, we obtain

\displaystyle\inf_{T}\,\sup_{|\theta|<\delta}\,\mathbb{E}_{\theta}|T(X)-\psi(\theta)|^{2}\geq\inf_{\nu\in\mathcal{V}(-1,1)}\frac{\left(\int_{-1}^{1}I(u>0)\nu(u)\,du\right)^{2}}{\delta^{-2}\mathcal{I}(\nu)+n\int_{-1}^{1}\mathcal{I}(\delta u)\nu(u)\,du}.

We now provide a concrete lower bound given by (
13
). To make progress, we bound the denominator with the largest Fisher information
\mathcal{I}(t)
over the local model
|t|<\delta
. When
\{P_{t}:t\in\Theta\}
is a location model, the Fisher information is a constant for all
t\in\Theta
. We then have that

\displaystyle\inf_{\nu\in\mathcal{V}(-1,1)}\,\frac{\left(\int_{-1}^{1}I(u>0)\nu(u)\,du\right)^{2}}{\delta^{-2}\mathcal{I}(\nu)+n\int_{-1}^{1}\mathcal{I}(\delta u)\nu(u)\,du}

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\nu\in\mathcal{V}(-1,1)}\,\frac{\left(\int_{-1}^{1}I(u>0)\nu(u)\,du\right)^{2}}{\delta^{-2}\mathcal{I}(\nu)+n\int_{-1}^{1}\{\sup_{t\in[-\delta,\delta]}\mathcal{I}(t)\}\nu(u)\,du}

\displaystyle=\sup_{a\in[0,1]}\,\inf_{\mathcal{I}(\nu)}\,\frac{a^{2}}{\delta^{-2}\mathcal{I}(\nu)+\sup_{t\in[-\delta,\delta]}\,n\mathcal{I}(t)}

(35)

where the infimum minimizes the Fisher information of the density
\nu
under the constraint that
\int_{0}^{1}\nu(t)\,dt=a
. In other words, we need to solve the following problem:

\displaystyle\inf_{\nu}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{(\nu^{\prime}(t))^{2}}{\nu(t)}dt\textrm{ i.e., }\nu\textrm{ is absolutely continuous, }\int_{0}^{1}\nu(t)\,dt=a\textrm{, and }\lim_{t\longrightarrow\pm\delta}\nu(t)=0.

The last constraint is introduced so
\nu
satisfies Definition
1
required for the van Trees inequality. Theorem 2.1 of
Ernst, (2017)
implies that the minimum Fisher information under the constraint satisfies

-2\eta^{\prime}(t)-\eta^{2}(t)=B_{1}I\{-1\leq t\leq 0\}+B_{2}I\{0<t\leq 1\}.

where
\eta(t)=\nu^{\prime}(t)/\nu(t)
and
B_{1}
,
B_{2}
are constants. The general solution of this first-order non-homogeneous ordinary differential equation is given by

\displaystyle\eta(t)=\nu^{\prime}(t)/\nu(t)=\begin{cases}\sqrt{B_{1}}\tan\left(\frac{\sqrt{B_{1}}}{2}(c_{1}-t)\right)&\text{where }-1\leq x\leq 0\\
\sqrt{B_{2}}\tan\left(\frac{\sqrt{B_{2}}}{2}(c_{2}-t)\right)&\text{where }0<x\leq 1\end{cases}

for constants
c_{1},c_{2}
. The general solution
\nu
is then given by

\displaystyle\nu(t)=\begin{cases}{C_{1}}\cos^{2}\left(\frac{\sqrt{B_{1}}}{2}(t-c_{1})\right)&\text{where }-1\leq x\leq 0\\
{C_{2}}\cos^{2}\left(\frac{\sqrt{B_{2}}}{2}(t-c_{2})\right)&\text{where }0<x\leq 1,\end{cases}

for constants
C_{1},C_{2}
. In other words, combining two squared cosine functions gives the minimum Fisher information prior
\nu
. A squared cosine function is known to minimize the Fisher information over distributions supported on
[-1,1]

(
Uhrmann-Klingen,, 1995
)
. In the current setting, the density
\nu
can have support over any subset of
[-1,1]
.

To simplify the derivation, we focus on a case when
C\equiv{C_{1}}={C_{2}}
,
B\equiv B_{1}=B_{2}
, and
c\equiv c_{1}=c_{2}
. In other words, we consider a location-scale family of squared cosine densities whose support is contained in
[-1,1]
such that
\supp(\nu)\subseteq[-1,1]
. Although we investigated other cases, this simplification still resulted in the best constant. We denote the left end of the support as
s_{-}\leq 0
and the right end as
s_{+}\geq 0
. The width of the support is defined as
(s_{+}-s_{-})
. First, by the assumption that the density vanishes towards the boundary, we have

\displaystyle C\cos^{2}\left(\frac{\sqrt{B}}{2}(s_{+}-c)\right)=0\Longrightarrow\sqrt{B}(s_{+}-c)=\pi\textrm{ and}

\displaystyle C\cos^{2}\left(\frac{\sqrt{B}}{2}(s_{-}-c)\right)=0\Longrightarrow\sqrt{B}(s_{-}-c)=-\pi.

Putting together, we have
\sqrt{B}(s_{+}-s_{-})=2\pi
and
c=(s_{+}-s_{-})/2
.
Next, by the constraint of
\int_{0}^{1}\nu(t)\,dt=a
, we have

\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{s_{+}}\cos^{2}\left(\frac{\sqrt{B}}{2}(x-c)\right)\,dt=\frac{Cs_{+}}{2}-\frac{C}{2\sqrt{B}}\sin(-c\sqrt{B})=a,\textrm{ and}

(36)

\displaystyle C\int_{s_{-}}^{0}\cos^{2}\left(\frac{\sqrt{B}}{2}(x-c)\right)\,dt=-\frac{Cs_{-}}{2}+\frac{C}{2\sqrt{B}}\sin(-c\sqrt{B})=1-a.

(37)

Putting together, we have
C=2/(s_{+}-s_{-})
.
Finally, we derive the Fisher information for this parametric family. First, for each
t\in[s_{-},s_{+}]
,

\displaystyle\frac{\{\nu^{\prime}(t)\}^{2}}{\nu(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{BC^{2}\cos^{2}\left(\frac{\sqrt{B}}{2}(t-c)\right)\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\sqrt{B}}{2}(t-c)\right)}{C\cos^{2}\left(\frac{\sqrt{B}}{2}(t-c)\right)}=BC\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\sqrt{B}}{2}(t-c)\right).

This implies that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(\nu)=\int_{s_{-}}^{s^{+}}BC\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\sqrt{B}}{2}(t-c)\right)\,dt=\frac{BC}{2}(s_{+}-s_{-})=\frac{4\pi^{2}}{(s_{+}-s_{-})^{2}}.

(38)

Hence, the Fisher information only depends on the width of the support. It thus remains to characterize the width of a squared cosine prior under the constraint
\int_{0}^{1}\nu(t)\,dt=a
. Plugging in the reduced expressions into (
36
) and (
37
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{Cs_{+}}{2}-\frac{C}{2\sqrt{B}}\sin(-c\sqrt{B})=a

\displaystyle\implies\frac{s_{+}}{s_{+}-s_{-}}-\frac{\sin\left(-\pi\frac{s_{+}+s_{-}}{s_{+}-s_{-}}\right)}{2\pi}=a\textrm{, and}

\displaystyle-\frac{Cs_{-}}{2}+\frac{C}{2\sqrt{B}}\sin(-c\sqrt{B})=1-a

\displaystyle\implies-\frac{s_{-}}{s_{+}-s_{-}}+\frac{\sin\left(-\pi\frac{s_{+}+s_{-}}{s_{+}-s_{-}}\right)}{2\pi}=1-a.

Putting together, we obtain

\displaystyle Y_{a}-\frac{\sin\left(-\pi Y_{a}\right)}{\pi}=2a-1\,\textrm{ where }\,Y_{a}=\frac{s_{+}+s_{-}}{s_{+}-s_{-}}.

(39)

This expression is known as the Kepler equation and there is no closed-form solution for the inverse problem
(
Kepler,, 1609
)
. We thus provide a computational method to estimate the smallest Fisher information given
Y_{a}
for each
a\in[0,1]
. Once
Y_{a}
is estimated from
a
, it follows that

\displaystyle\frac{s_{+}+s_{-}}{s_{+}-s_{-}}=Y_{a}\Longleftrightarrow(1-Y_{a})s_{+}=-(1+Y_{a})s_{-}.

This concludes that

\displaystyle s_{+}-s_{-}=\frac{2}{Y_{a}+1}s_{+}=-\frac{2}{1-Y_{a}}s_{-}.

As the minimum Fisher information is achieved by maximizing the width (see Equation
38
), we take
s_{+}=1
when
a>0.5
or
s_{-}=-1
when
a\leq 0.5
. Denoting by
W_{a}
the width of the support of
\nu
that minimizes the Fisher information for each
a\in[0,1]
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\inf\left\{\mathcal{I}(\nu):\int_{0}^{1}\nu(t)\,dt=a\right\}=4\pi^{2}/W_{a}^{2}\hskip 9.24994pt\textrm{where}\hskip 9.24994ptW_{a}=\begin{cases}2/(Y_{a}+1)&\textrm{ when }a>0.5\\
2/(1-Y_{a})&\textrm{ when }a\leq 0.5\end{cases}

We also note that (
39
) is an odd function and the expression for
W_{a}
is an even function. Hence, we can modify them to

\displaystyle Y_{a}-\frac{\sin\left(-\pi Y_{a}\right)}{\pi}=|2a-1|\,\textrm{ and }\,W_{a}=2/(Y_{a}+1).

Figure 2:
Visual representation of the minimum Fisher information priors for the location-scale family of squared cosine densities. The left panel displays an example of the minimum Fisher information prior when
a=0.75
. The shaded area corresponds to the constraint
\int_{0}^{1}\nu(t)\,dt
, which is
0.75
in this case. The center panel displays the relationship between the constraint
a
(or the shaded area in the left panel) and the width of the support of the minimum Fisher information priors. The dot in the plot corresponds to the density with
a=0.75
displayed in the left panel. The right panel displays the relationship between the moment constraint
a
and the minimum Fisher information. The dot on the plot again corresponds to the density with
a=0.75
displayed in the left panel. The minimum Fisher information is achieved by setting
a=0.5
; however, this is not necessarily the least-favorable prior for minimax lower bounds.

Finally, plugging this expression back into the van Trees inequality given by (
35
), we conclude

\displaystyle\inf_{T}\,\sup_{|\theta|<\delta}\,\mathbb{E}_{\theta}|T(X)-\psi(\theta)|^{2}\geq\sup_{a\in[0,1]}\,\frac{a^{2}}{4\pi^{2}\delta^{-2}W_{a}^{2}+n\sup_{t\in[-\delta,\delta]}\mathcal{I}(t)}.

We note that
a=0.5
corresponds to the minimum Fisher information prior while this may not necessarily maximize the minimax lower bound due to the second term in the denominator. Figure
2
provides a visual representation of the squared cosine priors and the minimum Fisher information.

Appendix G
Additional derivations related to examples

In this section, we provide the remaining derivation for Examples
3
and
4
from the main text.

G.1
Proof of Lemma
13

Suppose
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
is drawn from
P_{\theta_{0}}
that belongs to a local model
\{P_{\theta}:|\theta|<\delta,\theta\in\mathbb{R}\}
for fixed
\delta>0
. We assume that this model is Hellinger differentiable with the Fisher information
\mathcal{I}(t)
for
t\in(-\delta,\delta)
. Let
\psi(P_{\theta})=\max(0,\theta^{\alpha})
for
0<\alpha\leq 1
.

For fixed
\delta>0
, we consider the diffeomorphism for the local univariate parameter set
\{\theta:|\theta-\theta_{0}|<\delta\}
defined as
\varphi(t):=\theta_{0}+\delta\varphi_{0}(t)
where
\varphi_{0}:\mathbb{R}\mapsto(-1,1)
. As Theorem
7
holds for any choice of diffeomorphism, we further assume that
\varphi_{0}(0)=0
,
\|\varphi^{\prime}_{0}\|_{\infty}<C
for some constant, and
\varphi_{0}
is an increasing function. The derivative of this mapping is
\varphi^{\prime}(t)=\delta\varphi_{0}^{\prime}(t)
. We now apply Theorem
7
to the composite function
(\psi\circ\varphi):=t\mapsto\max(0,\varphi^{\alpha}(t))
,

\displaystyle\inf_{T}\,\sup_{|\theta-\theta_{0}|<\delta}\,\mathbb{E}_{\theta}|T(X)-\psi(\varphi(\theta))|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\limsup_{h\longrightarrow 0}\,\left[\frac{\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\psi(\varphi(t))-\psi(\varphi(t-h))\right)\,dQ(t)\right|}{2H\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{0},\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h}\right)}-\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\psi(\varphi(t))-\psi(\varphi(t-h))\right|^{2}\,dQ(t)\right)^{1/2}\right]^{2}_{+}.

By Lemma
18
in Supplementary Material, we have

\displaystyle H^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{0},\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h})

\displaystyle=\frac{h^{2}}{4}\left(\mathcal{I}(Q)+\delta^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\{\varphi_{0}^{\prime}(t)\}^{2}\,n\mathcal{I}(\theta_{0}+\delta\varphi_{0}(t))\,dQ(t)\right)+o(h^{2}).

We now analyze the behavior of
\psi(\varphi(t))-\psi(\varphi(t-h))
as
|h|\longrightarrow 0
for different values of
\theta_{0}
. As the functional
\psi
is directionally differentiable, we need to consider
h\longrightarrow 0
from left and right.

Case 1
\theta_{0}<0
:

When
h\longrightarrow 0
from right, or
h\downarrow 0
, we have
I\{t:\theta_{0}+\delta\varphi_{0}(t-h)>0\}\Longrightarrow I\{t:\theta_{0}+\delta\varphi_{0}(t)>0\}
by the monotonicity of
\varphi_{0}
. We also observe that

\displaystyle\theta_{0}+\delta\varphi_{0}(t-h)>0\Longleftrightarrow\theta_{0}>-\delta\varphi_{0}(t-h)\Longleftrightarrow|\theta_{0}|/\delta<\varphi_{0}(t-h).

We then have

\displaystyle\psi(\varphi(t))-\psi(\varphi(t-h))

\displaystyle=\psi(\varphi(t))I\{t:|\theta_{0}|/\delta<\varphi_{0}(t)\}-\psi(\varphi(t-h))I\{t:|\theta_{0}|/\delta<\varphi_{0}(t-h)\}

\displaystyle=\left\{\varphi(t)^{\alpha}-\varphi(t-h)^{\alpha}\right\}I\{t:|\theta_{0}|/\delta<\varphi_{0}(t-h)\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\varphi(t)^{\alpha}I\{t:\varphi_{0}(t-h)\leq|\theta_{0}|/\delta<\varphi_{0}(t)\}.

We denote by
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}
constants
0\leq\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\leq 1
, possibly depening on
t
. By the mean value theorem,

\displaystyle\varphi(t)^{\alpha}-\varphi(t-h)^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\alpha\{(1-\lambda_{1})\varphi(t)+\lambda_{1}\varphi(t-h)\}^{\alpha-1}\varphi^{\prime}(t-\lambda_{2}h)h

\displaystyle=\delta\alpha[\theta_{0}+\delta\{(1-\lambda_{1})\varphi_{0}(t)+\lambda_{1}\varphi_{0}(t-h)\}]^{\alpha-1}\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(t-\lambda_{2}h)h.

Therefore, as
h\downarrow 0
, we have

\displaystyle\varphi(t)^{\alpha}-\varphi(t-h)^{\alpha}=\delta\alpha\{\theta_{0}+\delta\varphi_{0}(t)\}^{\alpha-1}\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(t)h+o(h),

which follows by the continuity of
\varphi_{0}
and
\varphi^{\prime}_{0}
. Similarly as
h\downarrow 0
, we have

\displaystyle\varphi(t)^{\alpha}I\{t:\varphi_{0}(t-h)\leq|\theta_{0}|/\delta<\varphi_{0}(t)\}

\displaystyle=\varphi(t)^{\alpha}I\{t:\varphi_{0}(t)-h\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(t_{*})\leq|\theta_{0}|/\delta<\varphi_{0}(t)\}

\displaystyle=\varphi(t)^{\alpha}I\{t:-h\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(t_{*})\leq|\theta_{0}|/\delta<0\}

for some
t_{*}\in[t-h,t]
. For any fixed
\theta_{0},\varphi_{0}
and
\delta
, this term is zero for
h
small enough. Putting together, we conclude that, as
h\downarrow 0
,

\displaystyle\psi(\varphi(t))-\psi(\varphi(t-h))=\delta\alpha\{\theta_{0}+\delta\varphi_{0}(t)\}^{\alpha-1}\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(t)h\,I\{t:|\theta_{0}|/\delta<\varphi_{0}(t)\}+o(h).

We repeat the analysis for
h\longrightarrow 0
from left, or
h\uparrow 0
. The analysis is identical except now we have
I\{t:\theta_{0}+\delta\varphi_{0}(t)>0\}\Longrightarrow I\{t:\theta_{0}+\delta\varphi_{0}(t-h)>0\}
. All results remain the same for
h\uparrow 0
as well.

Finally, by plugging each term into the lower bound given by Theorem
7
and taking the limit in view of the dominated convergence theorem, we conclude that

\displaystyle\inf_{T}\,\sup_{|\theta-\theta_{0}|<\delta}\,\mathbb{E}_{\theta}|T(X)-\psi(\varphi(\theta))|^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\geq\limsup_{h\longrightarrow 0}\,\left[\frac{\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\psi(\varphi(t))-\psi(\varphi(t-h))\right)\,dQ(t)\right|}{2H\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{0},\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{h}\right)}-\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\psi(\varphi(t))-\psi(\varphi(t-h))\right|^{2}\,dQ(t)\right)^{1/2}\right]^{2}_{+}.

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\frac{\delta^{2}\alpha^{2}|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\{\theta_{0}+\delta\varphi_{0}(t)\}^{\alpha-1}\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(t)\,I\{t:|\theta_{0}|/\delta<\varphi_{0}(t)\}\,dQ(t)|^{2}}{\mathcal{I}(Q)+\delta^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\{\varphi_{0}^{\prime}(t)\}^{2}\,n\mathcal{I}(\theta_{0}+\delta\varphi_{0}(t))\,dQ(t)}.

As the upper bound does not involve
Q,\varphi_{0}
, we take the supremum over them. When
|\theta_{0}|>\delta
, the indicator in the numerator evaluates zero for all values of
t\in\mathbb{R}
. The local minimax lower bound becomes zero in such cases.

Case 2
\theta_{0}>0
:

Similar to the case with
\theta_{0}<0
, we consider
h\longrightarrow 0
from two directions. The results are analogous thus we only provide the argument for
h\downarrow 0
. When
h\downarrow 0
, we have
I\{t:\theta_{0}-\delta\varphi_{0}(t)>0\}\Longrightarrow I\{t:\theta_{0}-\delta\varphi_{0}(t-h)>0\}
. We then have

\displaystyle\psi(\varphi(t))-\psi(\varphi(t-h))

\displaystyle=\left\{\varphi(t)^{\alpha}-\varphi(t-h)^{\alpha}\right\}I\{t:\varphi_{0}(t)<\theta_{0}/\delta\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-\varphi(t-h)^{\alpha}I\{t:\varphi_{0}(t-h)<\theta_{0}/\delta\leq\varphi_{0}(t)\}.

By the analogous argument from
\theta_{0}<0
, we can show that as
h\downarrow 0
,

\displaystyle\psi(\varphi(t))-\psi(\varphi(t-h))=\delta\alpha\{\theta_{0}+\delta\varphi_{0}(t)\}^{\alpha-1}\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(t)h\,I\{t:\varphi_{0}(t)<\theta_{0}/\delta\}+o(h).

In view of the dominated convergence theorem, we conclude that

\displaystyle\inf_{T}\,\sup_{|\theta-\theta_{0}|<\delta}\,\mathbb{E}_{\theta}|T(X)-\psi(\varphi(\theta))|^{2}\geq\frac{\delta^{2}\alpha^{2}|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\{\theta_{0}+\delta\varphi_{0}(t)\}^{\alpha-1}\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(t)\,I\{t:\varphi_{0}(t)<\theta_{0}/\delta\}\,dQ(t)|^{2}}{\mathcal{I}(Q)+\delta^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\{\varphi_{0}^{\prime}(t)\}^{2}\,n\mathcal{I}(\theta_{0}+\delta\varphi_{0}(t))\,dQ(t)}.

Case 3
\theta_{0}=0
:

The analysis is also similar to the previous two cases. After the analogous derivation, we arrive at

\displaystyle\inf_{T}\,\sup_{|\theta-\theta_{0}|<\delta}\,\mathbb{E}_{\theta}|T(X)-\psi(\varphi(\theta))|^{2}\geq\frac{\delta^{2}\alpha^{2}|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\{\theta_{0}+\delta\varphi_{0}(t)\}^{\alpha-1}\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(t)\,I\{t:\varphi_{0}(t)<\theta_{0}/\delta\}\,dQ(t)|^{2}}{\mathcal{I}(Q)+\delta^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\{\varphi_{0}^{\prime}(t)\}^{2}\,n\mathcal{I}(\theta_{0}+\delta\varphi_{0}(t))\,dQ(t)}.

By plugging in
\theta_{0}=0
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\inf_{T}\,\sup_{|\theta-\theta_{0}|<\delta}\,\mathbb{E}_{\theta}|T(X)-\psi(\varphi(\theta))|^{2}\geq\frac{\delta^{2\alpha}\alpha^{2}|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\{\varphi_{0}(t)\}^{\alpha-1}\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(t)\,I\{t:\varphi_{0}(t)>0\}\,dQ(t)|^{2}}{\mathcal{I}(Q)+\delta^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\{\varphi_{0}^{\prime}(t)\}^{2}\,n\mathcal{I}(\delta\varphi_{0}(t))\,dQ(t)}.

G.2
Proof of Lemma
14

Suppose
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}
is an IID observation from
P_{\theta_{0}}
, which belongs to a model
\{\text{Unif}(0,\theta)\,:\,0<\theta\}
.
The local parameter set we consider is given by
\Theta=(\theta_{0}-cn^{-1},\theta_{0}+cn^{-1})
, we define the diffeomorephism in the form of
\varphi(t):=\theta_{0}+cn^{-1}\varphi_{0}(t)
where
\varphi_{0}:\mathbb{R}\mapsto(-1,1)
is invertible and differentiable increasing function. Based on the analogous application of Theorem
7
as in Example
3
, we obtain, for any
h\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\inf_{T}\,\sup_{|\theta-\theta_{0}|<cn^{-1}}\,\mathbb{E}_{P^{n}_{\theta}}|T(X)-\theta|^{2}

\displaystyle=\inf_{T}\,\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\,\mathbb{E}_{P^{n}_{\varphi(t)}}|T(X)-\varphi(t)|^{2}

(40)

\displaystyle\geq\left[\frac{\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\varphi(t)-\varphi(t-h)\right)\,dQ(t)\right|}{2H\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}^{n}_{0},\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}^{n}_{h}\right)}-\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\varphi(t)-\varphi(t-h)\right|^{2}\,dQ(t)\right)^{1/2}\right]^{2}_{+}

For each
c
, we assume that
n
is a large enough constant such that
\theta_{0}-cn^{-1}>0
. By the mean value theorem, we have

\varphi(t)-\varphi(t-h)=cn^{-1}(\varphi_{0}(t)-\varphi_{0}(t-h))=cn^{-1}h\varphi_{0}^{\prime}(t+\lambda h)

for some
\lambda\in[0,1]
. The derivation thus focuses primarily on the Hellinger distance between
\mathrm{Unif}(0,\varphi(t))
and
\mathrm{Unif}(0,\varphi(t+h))
. First, the Hellinger distance associated with one observation is given by

\displaystyle 2-2\int dP^{1/2}_{\varphi(t)}\,dP^{1/2}_{\varphi(t+h)}

\displaystyle=2-2\frac{\varphi(t)}{\varphi^{1/2}(t)\varphi^{1/2}(t+h)}

\displaystyle=2-2\frac{\varphi^{1/2}(t)+\varphi^{1/2}(t+h)-\varphi^{1/2}(t+h)}{\varphi^{1/2}(t+h)}

\displaystyle=2\frac{\varphi^{1/2}(t+h)-\varphi^{1/2}(t)}{\varphi^{1/2}(t+h)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\{\lambda_{1}\varphi(t)+(1-\lambda_{1})\varphi(t+h)\}^{-1/2}\varphi^{\prime}(t+\lambda_{2}h)h}{\varphi^{1/2}(t+h)}

\displaystyle=\frac{c\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(t+\lambda_{2}h)h}{n\theta_{0}}+o(1/n).

Furthermore, by the tensorization property of the Hellinger distance, we have

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,H^{2}\left(P^{n}_{\varphi(t+h)},P^{n}_{\varphi(t)}\right)

\displaystyle=\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,\left\{2-2\left(1-\frac{H^{2}\left(P_{\varphi(t)},P_{\varphi(t+h)}\right)}{2}\right)^{n}\right\}

\displaystyle=2-2\exp\left(-\frac{c\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(t+\lambda_{2}h)h}{2\theta_{0}}\right).

The last step follows by
(1-x/n)^{n}\longrightarrow\ \exp(-x)
as
n\longrightarrow\infty
. Therefore we have

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,H^{2}\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}^{n}_{0},\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}^{n}_{h}\right)

\displaystyle=\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\left\{\,H^{2}(Q_{h},Q)+\int H^{2}\left(P^{n}_{\varphi(t+h)},P^{n}_{\varphi(t)}\right)\,dQ^{1/2}(t+h)\,dQ^{1/2}(t)\right\}

\displaystyle=2-2\int\exp\left(-\frac{c\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(t+\lambda h)h}{2\theta_{0}}\right)\,dQ^{1/2}(t+h)\,dQ^{1/2}(t).

Putting together, we have

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,\inf_{T}\,\sup_{|\theta-\theta_{0}|<cn^{-1}}\,n^{2}\mathbb{E}_{P^{n}_{\theta}}|T(X)-\theta|^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\geq\bigg[\frac{ch\left|\int\left(\varphi_{0}^{\prime}(t+\lambda h)\right)\,dQ(t)\right|}{2\left(2-2\int\exp\left(-\frac{c\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(t+\lambda h)h}{2\theta_{0}}\right)\,dQ^{1/2}(t+h)\,dQ^{1/2}(t)\right)^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt-ch\left(\int\{\varphi_{0}^{\prime}(t+\lambda h)\}^{2}\,dQ(t)\right)^{1/2}\bigg]^{2}_{+}.

Since the above inequality holds for any
h
, we denote by
h=(\theta_{0}/c)\eta
and it still holds for any
\eta
. Under this parameterization, we have

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,\inf_{T}\,\sup_{|\theta-\theta_{0}|<cn^{-1}}\,n^{2}\mathbb{E}_{P^{n}_{\theta}}|T(X)-\theta|^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\geq\bigg[\frac{\theta_{0}\eta\left|\int\left(\varphi_{0}^{\prime}(t+\lambda(\theta_{0}/c)\eta)\right)\,dQ(t)\right|}{2\left(2-2\int\exp\left(-\frac{\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(t+\lambda(\theta_{0}/c)\eta)\eta}{2}\right)\,dQ^{1/2}(t+(\theta_{0}/c)\eta)\,dQ^{1/2}(t)\right)^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt-\theta_{0}\eta\left(\int\{\varphi_{0}^{\prime}(t+\lambda(\theta_{0}/c)\eta)\}^{2}\,dQ(t)\right)^{1/2}\bigg]^{2}_{+}

for any
\eta
. Now, taking
c\longrightarrow\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\liminf_{c\longrightarrow\infty}\,\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,\inf_{T}\,\sup_{|\theta-\theta_{0}|<cn^{-1}}\,n^{2}\mathbb{E}_{P^{n}_{\theta}}|T(X)-\theta|^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\geq\sup_{\eta\in\mathbb{R},\,Q,\,\varphi_{0}}\,\theta_{0}^{2}\left[\frac{\eta\left|\int\varphi_{0}^{\prime}(t)\,dQ(t)\right|}{2\left(2-2\int\exp\left(-\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(t)\eta/2\right)\,dQ(t)\right)^{1/2}}-\eta\left(\int\{\varphi_{0}^{\prime}(t)\}^{2}\,dQ(t)\right)^{1/2}\right]^{2}_{+}.

The constant can be made strictly positive for certain choices of
Q
by the analogous argument as the proof of Lemma
10
. This concludes the claim.

G.3
On the constant for Example 4

As discussed in the main text, the optimal constant for the lower bound is known and is 1. However, the current expression involves the choice of
Q
and
\varphi_{0}
, and it is unclear whether the optimal constant can be obtained by certain choices of
Q
and
\varphi_{0}
. While we do not pursue this to the full extent, we provide some initial attempts in the following propositions.

Proposition 4
.

Consider a sequence of diffeomorphism
\varphi_{0}(t;\eta)
, which is indexed by
\eta>0
. Assuming that
\eta\varphi_{0}^{\prime}(t;\eta)\longrightarrow C
as
\eta\longrightarrow\infty
for some finite constant, we obtain

\displaystyle\liminf_{c\longrightarrow\infty}\,\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,\inf_{T}\,\sup_{|\theta-\theta_{0}|<cn^{-1}}\mathbb{E}_{P_{\theta}^{n}}n^{2}|T(X)-\theta|^{2}\geq C^{*}\theta_{0}^{2}

where
C^{*}\approx 0.0635^{2}
.

Proof of Proposition
4
.

Consider the following sequence of diffeomorphisms indexed by
\eta
such that

\eta\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(t;\eta)\longrightarrow C

as
\eta\longrightarrow\infty
. This is satisfied, for instance, by
2/\pi\arctan(t/\eta)
. Then the lower bound can be simplified as

\displaystyle\sup_{\eta\in\mathbb{R},\,Q,\,\varphi_{0}}\,\left[\frac{\eta\left|\int\varphi_{0}^{\prime}(t)\,dQ(t)\right|}{2\left(2-2\int\exp\left(-\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(t)\eta/2\right)\,dQ(t)\right)^{1/2}}-\eta\left(\int\{\varphi_{0}^{\prime}(t)\}^{2}\,dQ(t)\right)^{1/2}\right]^{2}_{+}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\geq\sup_{C}\,\left[\frac{C}{2\left(2-2\exp\left(-C/2\right)\right)^{1/2}}-C\right]^{2}_{+}\approx 0.0635^{2}

by optimizing for
C
.
∎

Proposition 5
.

Under the settings as Example
4
, Lemma
8
provides the following local asymptotic minimax lower bound:

\displaystyle\liminf_{c\longrightarrow\infty}\,\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,\inf_{T}\,\sup_{|\theta-\theta_{0}|<cn^{-1}}\mathbb{E}_{P_{\theta}^{n}}n^{2}|T(X)-\theta|^{2}\geq C^{*}\theta_{0}^{2}

where
C^{*}\approx 0.0558
.

Proof of Proposition
5
.

We now apply Lemma
8
to derive the optimal constant for the lower bound. Consider two points in parameter space
\theta_{0}
and
\theta_{0}+hn^{-1}
, and Lemma
8
implies

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,\sup_{|h|<c}\,n^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{0}+hn^{-1/2}}\big|T(X)-\psi(\theta_{0}+hn^{-1/2})\big|^{2}\geq\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,\left[\frac{1-H^{2}(P^{n}_{\theta_{0}+hn^{-1/2}},P^{n}_{\theta_{0}})}{4}\right]_{+}h^{2}

(41)

for all
|h|<c
. An analogous derivation to Example 3 will yield

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,H^{2}\left(P^{n}_{\theta_{0}+hn^{-1}},P^{n}_{\theta_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle=2-2\exp\left(-\frac{h}{2\theta_{0}}\right)

for
n
IID observations from two uniform distributions. Putting together, it follows that

\displaystyle\liminf_{c\longrightarrow\infty}\,\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,\sup_{|h|<\infty}\,n^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{0}+hn^{-1/2}}\big|T(X)-\psi(\theta_{0}+hn^{-1/2})\big|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sup_{|h|<c}\,\left[-\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{2}\exp\left(-\frac{h}{2\theta_{0}}\right)\right]_{+}h^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sup_{|\eta|<\infty}\,4\theta_{0}^{2}\left[-\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{2}\exp(-\eta)\right]_{+}\eta^{2}

\displaystyle\approx 0.0558\,\theta_{0}^{2}

where we parameterize
h=2\eta\theta_{0}
and optimize for
\eta
to obtain the result.
∎

Appendix H
Derivation of the risk of estimators

In this section, we provide exact upper bounds and lower bounds for the truncated Gaussian mean estimation. To recall, we consider the following estimation problem:

\displaystyle\sup_{|\theta|<\delta}\,n\mathbb{E}_{\theta}|T(X)-\max(\theta,0)|^{2}

where we observe
n\geq 1
IID observations
X:=X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
from
N(0,1)
. We define a local model as
\{N(\theta,1):|\theta|<\delta\}
for
\delta>0
.

H.1
Lower bound under Gaussian prior and arctan diffeomorphism

In Example
3
(iii) with
\alpha=1
, we have derived that

\displaystyle\inf_{T}\,\sup_{|\theta|<\delta}\,n\mathbb{E}_{\theta}|T(X)-\psi(P_{\theta})|^{2}\geq\sup_{Q,\varphi_{0}}\,\frac{n\delta^{2}|\mathbb{E}_{Q}\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(t)\,I\{t:\varphi_{0}(t)>0\}|^{2}}{\mathcal{I}(Q)+\delta^{2}\mathbb{E}_{Q}\{\varphi_{0}^{\prime}(t)\}^{2}\,\mathcal{I}(\delta\varphi_{0}(t))}.

The Fisher information of distributions in our local model is given by
\mathcal{I}(\theta)=n
for all
|\theta|<\delta
under
n
IID observations. To simplify the derivation, we further restrict the choice of priors and diffeomorphism to be

\displaystyle Q\in\{N(\mu,\sigma^{2}):(\mu,\sigma)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\}\textrm{ and }\varphi_{0}\in\{t\mapsto\pi/2\arctan(t/\eta):\eta>0\}.

We define
\xi_{1}:=\mu/\eta
and
\xi_{2}:=\sigma/\eta
. Since
\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(t)=2(\pi\eta)^{-1}(1+(t/\eta)^{2})^{-1}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{Q}\{\varphi_{0}^{\prime}(t)\}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int\frac{4}{\pi^{2}\eta^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{1+(t/\eta)^{2}}\right)^{2}\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp(-(t-\mu)^{2}/(2\sigma^{2}))\,dt

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{\pi^{2}\eta^{2}}\int\left(\frac{1}{1+(\xi_{1}+u\xi_{2})^{2}}\right)^{2}\phi(u)\,du

where
\phi
is a density function of a standard normal distribution. Similarly,

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}_{Q}\varphi_{0}^{\prime}(t)I(\varphi_{0}(t)>0)|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\int\frac{2}{\pi\eta}\left(\frac{1}{1+(t/\eta)^{2}}\right)\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp(-(t-\mu)^{2}/(2\sigma^{2}))I(t>0)\,dt\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{\pi^{2}\eta^{2}}\left|\int\left(\frac{1}{1+(\mu/\eta+u\sigma/\eta)^{2}}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp(-u^{2}/2)I(\mu+u\sigma>0)\,du\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{\pi^{2}\eta^{2}}\left|\int\left(\frac{1}{1+(\xi_{1}+u\xi_{2})^{2}}\right)I(\xi_{1}+u\xi_{2}>0)\phi(u)\,du\right|^{2}.

Using the fact that
\mathcal{I}(Q)=\sigma^{-2}
, the lower bound becomes

\displaystyle\frac{n\delta^{2}|\mathbb{E}_{Q}\varphi_{0}^{\prime}(t)I(t>0)|^{2}}{(1/\sigma^{2})+\delta^{2}n\mathbb{E}_{Q}\{\varphi_{0}^{\prime}(t)\}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{4n\xi_{2}^{2}\left|\int\left(1+(\xi_{1}+u\xi_{2})^{2}\right)^{-1}I(u>-\xi_{1}/\xi_{2})\phi(u)\,du\right|^{2}}{\pi^{2}\delta^{-2}+4n\int\left(1+(\xi_{1}+u\xi_{2})^{2}\right)^{-2}\phi(u)\,du}

\displaystyle=\frac{4n\xi_{2}^{2}\left|\E\left[\left(1+(\xi_{1}+Z\xi_{2})^{2}\right)^{-1}I(Z>-\xi_{1}/\xi_{2})\right]\right|^{2}}{\pi^{2}\delta^{-2}+4n\E\left[\left(1+(\xi_{1}+Z\xi_{2})^{2}\right)^{-2}\right]}

where
Z\overset{d}{=}N(0,1)
. We conclude the claim by optimizing over
(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
for given
\delta>0
and
n\geq 1
.

H.2
Proof of Proposition
2

In this section, we provide the exact local risks of the following plug-in estimators, defined as

\displaystyle S_{n}^{\text{plug-in}}

\displaystyle:=\psi(\widehat{\theta}_{\text{MLE}})\hskip 9.24994pt\text{where}\hskip 9.24994pt\widehat{\theta}_{\text{MLE}}:=\overline{X}_{n}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i},\hskip 9.24994pt\text{and}

\displaystyle S_{n}^{\text{pre-test}}

\displaystyle:=\psi(\widehat{\theta}_{\text{pre-test}})\hskip 9.24994pt\text{where}\hskip 9.24994pt\widehat{\theta}_{\text{pre-test}}:=\begin{cases}\overline{X}_{n}&\text{If}\hskip 9.24994pt|\overline{X}_{n}|\geq n^{-1/4}\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Proof of Proposition
2
.

First, we consider the plug-in MLE. By splitting the risk into two cases, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{|\theta|<\delta}\,n\mathbb{E}_{\theta}|S_{n}^{\text{plug-in}}-\max(\theta,0)|^{2}=\max\left\{\sup_{0\leq\theta<\delta}\,n\mathbb{E}_{\theta}|S_{n}^{\text{plug-in}}-\theta|^{2},\sup_{-\delta<\theta<0}\,n\mathbb{E}_{\theta}|S_{n}^{\text{plug-in}}|^{2}\right\}.

For the first case, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq\theta<\delta}\,n\mathbb{E}_{\theta}|S_{n}^{\text{plug-in}}-\theta|^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\sup_{0\leq\theta<\delta}\,n\left\{\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[|\overline{X}_{n}-\theta|^{2}I(\overline{X}_{n}\geq 0)]+\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[\theta^{2}I(\overline{X}_{n}<0)]\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\sup_{0\leq\theta<\delta}\,\left\{\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[|n^{1/2}(\overline{X}_{n}-\theta)|^{2}I(n^{1/2}(\overline{X}_{n}-\theta)\geq-n^{1/2}\theta)]+n\theta^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[I(n^{1/2}(\overline{X}_{n}-\theta)<-n^{1/2}\theta)]\right\}.

Since
Z:=n^{1/2}(\overline{X}_{n}-\theta)\overset{d}{=}N(0,1)
, we conclude

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq\theta<\delta}\,n\mathbb{E}_{\theta}|S_{n}^{\text{plug-in}}-\theta|^{2}=\sup_{0\leq\theta<\delta}\,\left\{\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[Z^{2}I(Z\geq-n^{1/2}\theta)]+n\theta^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[I(Z<-n^{1/2}\theta)]\right\}.

Next, we claim that

\displaystyle\sup_{-\delta<\theta\leq 0}\,n\mathbb{E}_{\theta}|S_{n}^{\text{plug-in}}|^{2}\leq\sup_{0\leq\theta<\delta}\,\mathbb{E}_{\theta}|S_{n}^{\text{plug-in}}-\theta|^{2},

(42)

that is, the risk is always greater when
\theta\geq 0
than
\theta<0
. Following an analogous argument from
\theta\geq 0
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{-\delta<\theta\leq 0}\,n\mathbb{E}_{\theta}|S_{n}^{\text{plug-in}}|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sup_{-\delta<\theta\leq 0}\,n\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[\overline{X}_{n}^{2}I(\overline{X}_{n}\geq 0)]

\displaystyle=\sup_{-\delta\leq\theta<0}\,\E[(Z+n^{1/2}\theta)^{2}I(Z\geq-n^{1/2}\theta)]

where
Z\overset{d}{=}N(0,1)
. Denoting by
\eta=n^{1/2}\theta
and by
\phi
the density function of a standard Gaussian distribution, it follows, for any
\eta\leq 0
,

\displaystyle\E(Z+\eta)^{2}I(Z\geq-\eta)

\displaystyle=\int_{-\eta}^{\infty}(z+\eta)^{2}\phi(z)\,dz\leq\int_{-\eta}^{\infty}(z+\eta)^{2}\phi(z+\eta)\,dz=\int_{0}^{\infty}\tilde{z}^{2}\phi(\tilde{z})\,d\tilde{z}.

The middle inequality follows since the density
\phi(z)
is non-increasing on
0\leq z
and thus
\phi(z)\leq\phi(z+\eta)
for any
\eta\leq 0
. The last quantity is equivalent to
\E[Z^{2}I(Z\geq 0)]
hence the risk of the estimator for any
\eta\leq 0
is upper bounded by the case when
\eta=0
, or equivalently when
\theta=0
. Therefore, equation (
42
) is implied as desired. This concludes the claim.

For the pre-test estimator, the derivation is almost analogous except that we have

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq\theta<\delta}\,n\mathbb{E}_{\theta}|S_{n}^{\text{pre-test}}-\theta|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sup_{0\leq\theta<\delta}\,n\left\{\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[|\overline{X}_{n}-\theta|^{2}I(\overline{X}_{n}\geq n^{-1/4})]+\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[\theta^{2}I(\overline{X}_{n}<n^{-1/4})]\right\}

\displaystyle=\sup_{0\leq\theta<\delta}\,\left\{\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[Z^{2}I(Z\geq n^{1/4}-n^{1/2}\theta)]+n\theta^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[I(Z<n^{1/4}-n^{1/2}\theta)]\right\}.

The remaining derivation is omitted.
∎

Appendix I
Connection to the modulus of continuity

We discuss the connections between Theorem
7
and existing minimax lower bounds in terms of the Hellinger distance. Previous works by
Donoho and Liu, (1987)
;
Donoho and Liu, (1991)
provide a geometric interpretation of the minimax rate through
the modulus of continuity
. We define the modulus of continuity of a real-valued functional
\psi
with respect to the Helligner distance as

\omega(\varepsilon):=\left\{\sup_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in\Theta_{0}}\,|\psi(P_{\theta_{1}})-\psi(P_{\theta_{2}})|\,:\,H^{2}(P_{\theta_{1}},P_{\theta_{2}})\leq\varepsilon^{2}\right\}.

This quantity captures the maximum fluctuation of functionals evaluated at sufficiently “similar” distributions. We momentarily focus on estimating
\psi(P_{0})
based on
n
IID observations
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
drawn from
P_{0}
. The minimax risk is considered for the supremum over a local model
\mathcal{P}_{n}:=\{P:H^{2}(P_{0},P)\leq n^{-1}\}
, which is a set of distributions concentrated at
P_{0}
, analogous to the setting of the LAM theorem. Section 9.4 of
Donoho and Liu, (1987)
proves that, under additional assumptions, for any
n
sufficiently large,

\displaystyle\inf_{T}\,\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}_{n}}\,n\mathbb{E}_{P}|T(X)-\psi(P)|^{2}>\frac{1}{16}\left(\frac{\omega(n^{-1/2})}{n^{-1/2}}\right)^{2}.

(43)

Donoho and Liu, (1987)
also shows that, under the setting of the LAM theorem (Theorem
1
) and
\psi(P_{\theta})=\theta
, it holds

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\longrightarrow\infty}\,\frac{1}{16}\left(\frac{\omega(n^{-1/2})}{n^{-1/2}}\right)^{2}=\frac{1}{4\mathcal{I}(\theta_{0})}.

(44)

Hence, the non-asymptotic minimax lower bound given by (
43
) converges to
1/4
the optimal constant according to the LAM theorem. It may be tempting to multiply the lower bound of (
43
) by
4
, that is, to consider the sequence
4^{-1}\{n^{1/2}\omega(n^{-1/2})\}^{2}
. Although this sequence converges to the correct constant as
n\longrightarrow\infty
, it is an invalid lower bound as there exists an estimator that violates this inequality, provided in Section 8.4 of
Donoho and Liu, (1987)
.
Donoho and Liu, (1987)
conjectured that a different approach is necessary to develop a sequence of non-asymptotic minimax lower bounds that converge to the correct limit. This result is provided by Theorem
7
. Our result is similar to
Donoho and Liu, (1987)
in the sense that both define local models relative to the Hellinger distance; however, they differ as we consider the mixture of distribution over the Hellinger ball instead of two distributions. It is also worth noting that an analogous non-asymptotic result in terms of the two-point modulus of continuity is studied by
Chen, (1997)
without particular focus on a sharp constant.

Finally, similar objects as the Hellinger modulus of continuity have been considered in the econometrics literature. When the local asymptotic minimax lower bound, such as the right-hand term of Theorem
7
, is bounded away from zero, the functional estimation is called
ill-posed

(
Pötscher,, 2002
;
Forchini and Hillier,, 2005
)
. This precludes the existence of a (locally) uniformly consistent estimator of
\psi(P_{0})
in the nonparametric model.
Pötscher, (2002)
and
Forchini and Hillier, (2005)
define the modulus of continuity in the total variation distance as

\omega(\varepsilon):=\left\{\sup_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in\Theta}|\psi(P_{\theta_{1}})-\psi(P_{\theta_{2}})|\,:\,\textrm{TV}(P_{\theta_{1}},P_{\theta_{2}})\leq\varepsilon\right\}

where the total variation distance is defined as

\displaystyle\textrm{TV}(P_{0},P_{1}):=\sup_{A\in\mathcal{A}}|P_{0}(A)-P_{1}(A)|,

with the supremum over all Borel measurable sets
A
.
Theorem 2.1 of
Pötscher, (2002)
provides the sufficient condition under which the following holds:

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\longrightarrow 0}\,\inf_{T}\,\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}(\varepsilon)}\,\mathbb{E}_{P}|T(X)-\psi(P)|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\lim_{\varepsilon\longrightarrow 0}\,\frac{1}{4}\omega(\varepsilon)^{2}.

where
\mathcal{P}(\varepsilon):=\{P:\mathrm{TV}(P_{0},P)\leq\varepsilon\}
.
Pötscher, (2002)
and
Forchini and Hillier, (2005)
analyze concrete problems where the lower bound is bounded away from zero. We note that there is no significant loss in considering the TV- or Hellinger-moduli as both distances define the same topology of the space of probability measures. However, our result also differs from
Pötscher, (2002)
and
Forchini and Hillier, (2005)
as we focus on the non-asymptotic results as well as the optimal constant. When the asymptotic lower bound of Theorem
7
is bounded away from zero, it can be considered as the measure of
ill-posedness
. We defer the corresponding analysis to future works.

Section
6
provides the detail of the simulation study. Figure
2
below shows the same phenomena as Figure
1
by fixing
\delta
and varying the sample size
n
.

Appendix K
Further remarks on the present results

K.1
Non-pathwise differentiable functionals

While we demonstrate the application of the proposed lower bounds to derive the semiparametric efficiency bound for pathwise differentiable functionals, the main results from Section
3
still hold for general non-pathwise differentiable functionals. First, by taking
\Phi
as a collection of pathwise differentiable functionals, Lemma
4
implies

\displaystyle\sup_{g\in\mathcal{T}_{P_{0}}}\,\inf_{T}\,\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\,\mathbb{E}_{\theta}|T(X)-\psi(P_{\theta,g})|^{2}

\displaystyle\,\,\geq\sup_{g\in\mathcal{T}_{P_{0}}}\,\sup_{\phi\in\Phi,\,Q\in\mathcal{Q}(\Theta)}\,\left[\left(\int\,\mathbb{E}_{\theta}|T(X)-\phi(P_{\theta,g})|^{2}\,dQ\right)^{1/2}-\left(\int\|\psi(P_{\theta,g})-\phi(P_{\theta,g})\|^{2}dQ(\theta)\right)^{1/2}\right]_{+}^{2}

This suggests that the efficiency bound for non-pathwise differentiable functionals can be analyzed by first placing a prior over the local parametric paths and then deriving the Bayes risk for estimating
\phi
at the expense of the approximation error.

Alternatively, we can apply Theorem
7
to each parametric path and take the supremum over any generic tangent set
\mathcal{T}_{P_{0}}\subseteq L_{2}^{0}(P_{0})
. This implies

\displaystyle\sup_{g\in\mathcal{T}_{P_{0}}}\,\inf_{T}\,\sup_{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\,\mathbb{E}_{\theta}|T(X)-\psi(P_{\theta,g})|^{2}

\displaystyle\,\,\geq\sup_{g\in\mathcal{T}_{P_{0}}}\,\sup_{h\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left[\left(\frac{|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\psi(P_{t,g})-\psi(P_{t-h,g}\right)\,dQ(t)|^{2}}{4H^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{0,g},\mathbb{P}_{h,g})}\right)^{1/2}-\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\psi(P_{t,g})-\psi(P_{t-h,g})|^{2}\,dQ(t)\right)^{1/2}\right]^{2}_{+}

where

d\mathbb{P}_{0,g}(x,\theta):=dP_{\theta,g}(x)\,dQ(\theta)\hskip 9.24994pt\mbox{and}\hskip 9.24994ptd\mathbb{P}_{h,g}(x,\theta):=dP_{\theta+h,g}(x)\,dQ(\theta+h).

In the above non-asymptotic lower bound, the limiting behavior of
\psi(P_{t,g})-\psi(P_{t-h,g})
as
h\longrightarrow 0
is unspecified, allowing
\psi
to be non-smooth at
t
.

K.2
Non-convex sets

This manuscript extends the result to a general parameter set
\Theta_{0}\subseteq\Theta
via diffeomorphism.
Alternatively, we can use the fact that the supremum over
\theta\in\Theta_{0}
can be obtained by considering any two points
\theta_{0},\theta_{1}\in\Theta_{0}
and any smooth path
\gamma:=\gamma_{\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}:(-\infty,\infty)\mapsto\Theta_{0}
such that
\lim_{u\to-\infty}\gamma(u)=\theta_{0},\lim_{u\to\infty}\gamma(u)=\theta_{1}
. Let
\widetilde{\psi}(u):=\psi(\gamma(u))
for
u\in\mathbb{R}
,

d\mathbb{P}_{0}(x,u):=dP_{\gamma(u)}(x)dQ(u)\hskip 9.24994pt\mbox{and}\hskip 9.24994ptd\mathbb{P}_{h}(x,u):=dP_{\gamma(u+h)}(x)dQ(u+h),

where
Q
is a probability measure on
\mathbb{R}
.
Using this notation, we obtain

\displaystyle\inf_{T}\,\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{0}}\,\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\|T(X)-\psi(\theta)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt=\inf_{T}\,\sup_{\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}\,\sup_{\gamma}\,\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\,\mathbb{E}_{\gamma(u)}\|T(X)-\widetilde{\psi}(u)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\geq\sup_{\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}\,\sup_{\gamma}\,\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\,\left[\left(\frac{\|\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\widetilde{\psi}(u)-\widetilde{\psi}(u-h))\,dQ(u)\|^{2}}{4H^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{0},\mathbb{P}_{h})}\right)^{1/2}-\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\|\widetilde{\psi}(u)-\widetilde{\psi}(u-h)\|^{2}\,dQ(u)\right)^{1/2}\right]_{+}^{2}.

As a concrete example, suppose we are interested in finding a lower bound when
\Theta_{0}:=\{\theta:\,\rho(\theta-\theta_{0})\leq\delta\}
for some positive homogeneous norm function
\rho(\cdot)
, then one can take
\theta_{1}=\theta_{0}+\delta h
such that
\rho(h)\leq 1
and consider
\gamma(u)=\theta_{0}+\Phi(u)\delta h
where
\Phi(\cdot)
is the CDF of the standard normal distribution. This approach of taking a smooth path between two arbitrary points allows for the application of the results above to non-convex, but continuous, parameter spaces
\Theta_{0}
.
